Not yet in the watchdog's daily-job list. **IBKR Transaction History statement received 2026-09-19 (Aug 24–Sep 18, base currency SGD) — ground truth:** confirms every audit finding (assignment lines: MU sells 100@925 and 100@930 on 8/26, AAOI buy 100@107 and HOOD buy 100@106 and BMNR buy 100@24 on 8/28, INTC buy 100@91 on 8/31, HOOD sell 100@107 and INTC sell 100@90 on 9/4, PLTR buy 200@167.5 on 9/11, AMAT sell 100@437.5 and HOOD sell 300@116 on 9/18; AAOI: bought 100@107.65 8/24, assigned +100@107 8/28, sold 100@107.10 8/31, 100 remain). Also shows the DB `trades` table is missing some partial fills (HOOD 8/24: call buy-back is 3 contracts but the row says 1; stock sells 100+101+99 but only 100 recorded). **Commission backfill:** 110/110 prod trade ids matched to statement Buy/Sell lines (108 exact, 3 lines folded into the nearest same-instrument row), commissions converted SGD->USD per line from the implied FX (gross / (qty x mult x price)); total USD 106.74. SQL at `tmp/commission_backfill_from_ibkr_statement.sql` (gitignored, one transaction, `WHERE commission IS NULL`), to be run by Marcelo via `heroku pg:psql -f`. Assignment lines (call-away sells carry a small commission, ~USD 6 total) have no trade rows and are not in it. Unverified: the currency IBKR's live `commissionReport` uses (handler stores the raw number, assumes USD) — check on the first live fill. **Commission backfill run on prod by Marcelo 2026-09-19** (110/110 trades now have a commission, total USD 106.74, verified). **AAOI/PLTR stock-leg fixes run on prod 2026-09-19** (`tmp/fix_aaoi_pltr_stock_legs.sql`: PLTR leg -> 200 sh @167.50 exit 167.43; AAOI sold lot entry 107.65 exit 107.10, assigned lot 107.00/107.00; realized P&L 28,636.65 -> 28,382.23, matching the rolled-back dev test). **Net-of-commission P&L built 2026-09-19 (committed, deploys with the next push):** finding first — IBKR's leg `entry_price` (avgCost) already embeds the OPENING commission (entry minus fill matches the commission on 62 legs; ~$93 of the $106.74), exit prices are raw fills, so realized P&L now = gross spread minus commissions of the leg's CLOSING trades only (~$13.92 to date), never the opening ones (would double count). One shared SQL fragment, `src/lib/legRealizedPnlSql.ts`, now replaces the copy-pasted formula in `positionQueries.ts` (3x), `strategyPeriodPnl.ts` (2x); `positionEvents.ts` does the same arithmetic in JS. Verified on dev after applying the commission backfill: realized total 28,382.23 -> 28,368.31 (-13.92; premium -7.66, stock -6.26), strategy-period totals shift by the same -13.92, Positions Closed totals row and API show it, no console errors. Unrealized P&L is unchanged (future closing cost unknown); nightly snapshots left as they are; the ~USD 6 of commissions on assignment sales (no trade rows) is not included. Positions `P&L $` header tooltip explains the net basis. Approved by Marcelo.
- **Ticker Detail: "History (N)" card removed, 2026-09-20 (front end, uncommitted).** The collapsible list of a ticker's closed positions (Strategy / Structure / Opened / Closed / Realized P&L) is gone from the modal at Marce's request; the "IV History" chart is a different section and stays, and the Wheel cycle card still shows for tickers that only have closed positions (the modal's closed-positions check now exists only for that). Closed positions remain visible on the Positions screen's Closed view.
- **Price Performance redesign, 2026-09-20 (both repos, uncommitted, not deployed).** *Plain summary:* the page used to ask IBKR for a year of daily prices for every shortlisted ticker (~18) on every page load just to draw two trend columns, so it took 30-60+ s and hung under a slow Gateway, and every reload piled another copy on top. All of that data is already stored locally by the nightly capture, so the page now reads it from the database and asks IBKR for nothing. *Design (approved by Marce, decisions 1-6):* three tiers by rate of change — (1) end-of-day facts (last close, reference closes, ranges, IV, MACD/MA trends) from `daily_price_bars` in ONE request, (2) live price only over the existing multiplexed stream, (3) live % changes computed in the browser from the live price and the reference closes delivered once with tier 1.
- **IBKR reads:** page load **0** (was ~18 historical requests per load, multiplied by reloads and tabs). The only page-triggered reads are the explicit **Refresh daily data** button, and only for tickers whose latest completed bar is behind (0 when current), single-flight, 2-minute cooldown, batches of 4, run through `runJob` as `price_bars_refresh` (System Health history, Pulse event, `job_completed` notification, Telegram on total failure). The nightly `daily_market_data_capture` (9 PM UTC) is unchanged and remains what keeps the data current.
- **Backend:** `GET /price-performance` now returns `{ tickers, meta }` in ~35 ms (was 27 s warm / minutes cold), with Express ETag so an unchanged repeat load is a 304 (~2 ms, 0 bytes) and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`; `lib/pricePerformanceSnapshot.ts` (one bars query for every ticker's trend history, IV metrics as before, 60 s in-process memo + single-flight, invalidated when a refresh finishes), `lib/priceTrends.ts` (the same `computeMacd`/`computeMovingAverages`/`buildTrendLabel` calculation as before, unit-tested for exact equality with the old code), `lib/priceBarsRefresh.ts`, `POST /price-performance/refresh`. **`GET /price-performance/trends` is deleted.** `GET /current-prices/stream` now sends `{ SYMBOL: price|null }` only (no per-connect SQL, no recomputation). New `lastCompletedSessionDate()` in `marketSessionStatus.ts` uses the existing `market_calendar` table (holiday/weekend aware, falls back to weekdays outside its coverage) and the 16:00 ET close.
- **Semantic changes you approved:** trend "spot" is now the last COMPLETED close (was: last bar, which could be a partial in-progress bar left by a chart top-up or the 1:30 PM UTC trade-alert scan); the same completed-only rule now also defines "Last Close" and every reference close, so a partial today-bar can no longer show up as Last Close during market hours (a latent bug). Live % changes are the same `(price − reference) / reference × 100` formula, now in the browser (`src/lib/priceChange.ts`).
- **Front end:** `PricePerformancePage.tsx` rewritten — table renders from stored data at once; status strip ("Daily data as of the … close" + live status badge); Refresh button in the header (disabled with an explanatory tooltip when nothing is behind / during cooldown / while running, cooldown counts down by itself); amber banner + warning marker on any ticker whose latest bar is behind; every live cell ends in a defined state (streaming / no quote / unavailable / no price yet after 8 s) instead of a spinner; reloads on the `price_bars_refresh` / `daily_market_data_capture` `job_completed` notification, plus a 3 s poll only while a refresh runs; the stream is opened once and reopened only if the shortlist itself changes. Column set unchanged.
- **Verified:** live values identical to the old `/trends` output for all 18 tickers; refresh flow end to end against a deliberately deleted TLT bar (behind → banner/marker/button → refresh fetched exactly that 1 ticker in ~3 s → banner gone; second click `alreadyRunning`, click inside cooldown refused; the restored bar matched the saved one exactly); stream failing / hanging both end in a message; desktop dark, mobile light, no sideways page scroll. 92 backend tests total, 17 new (session walk-back incl. holidays and DST, freshness rule, percent formula, trend equality vs the old calculation).
- **Deploy:** no migration, no new environment variable. `market_calendar` should stay synced (`scripts/sync-market-calendar.ts`). Once deployed, the first thing worth eyeballing on prod is the "Daily data as of …" line matching the last trading day.
- **Deferred by decision:** the same "no live top-up for daily bars" rule for the Ticker Detail modal and trade-alert generation (`getCachedChartBars` still tops up when >30 s old); it would change the modal chart's last bar, so it needs its own approval. Trend history persistence (a `ticker_technicals` table) only if signal history is ever wanted.
- **Notification stream survives dyno restarts, 2026-09-20 (front end, uncommitted).** A browser `EventSource` that receives a non-200 answer — such as the Heroku router's 503 while the dyno restarts — is closed for good and never retries (verified), and the shared notification stream relied on the browser's auto-reconnect, so after a restart open tabs could silently stop getting toasts, `position_opened` refetches, presence and Pulse frames until reloaded. `src/api/notifications.ts` now recreates the connection whenever `readyState` is CLOSED after an error, retrying forever with backoff 1s, 2s, 5s, 10s, then 30s (each ±25% jitter, reset on a successful open); a drop that leaves it CONNECTING is still left to the browser. Safe to retry forever because a retry only asks for this cheap stream (the per-data streams still never retry, deliberately). Timers are cleared when a connection is retired or every listener leaves. Reproduced and verified in Playwright against the real app by intercepting the stream with a 503: BEFORE the fix one request, `readyState=2`, never recovered in 32s although the server was healthy from 12s; AFTER, requests at 0.4s, 1.6s, 4.0s, 9.6s (all 503) then 18.2s (server healthy) opened without a page reload. The full restart sequence (healthy stream, then the dyno kills it, then 503s, then recovery) also recovers (retries at 0.9s browser-side, then 2.1s, 4.6s, 9.6s, 18.7s), a `pg_notify` `position_closed` toasts over the recovered connection, and the Pulse swap scenario still passes (3 swaps, server connection count steady). **Known limitation:** notifications sent while a tab is disconnected are lost (same as any EventSource reconnect today) — the multiplexer's resubscribe step should trigger a refetch of dependent data. A 401 (expired session) also counts as a non-200, so an expired-session tab retries every 30s instead of dying; harmless but worth knowing. Testing gotchas: a Playwright wrapper around `window.EventSource` must copy the `CONNECTING/OPEN/CLOSED` constants or the app's `EventSource.CLOSED` check silently breaks; `browser_run_code_unsafe`'s `addInitScript` accumulates across calls in one browser, so close the browser between runs or events get recorded twice; `context.setOffline` does not cut an already-open stream (fulfil a finite `text/event-stream` body instead to simulate the dyno killing it).
- **Pulse animations fix, 2026-09-19 (front end, uncommitted).** Bug found while investigating the multiplexer: another session added `?pulses=1` to `/notifications/stream` (only the Pulse page wants the high-frequency topology frames) but chose it from `window.location.pathname` when the stream was first opened — and since the shared notification stream (`ca35031`) stays open for the whole session, a tab that loaded any other page and then navigated to Pulse never got `?pulses=1`, so the topology animation silently never fired (loading `/pulse` directly worked). `openNotificationStream(callback, { wantsPulses })` in `src/api/notifications.ts` now reconciles the shared connection: when Pulse subscribes/unsubscribes it opens a replacement with/without `?pulses=1`, the replacement takes over delivery the moment it opens and only then retires the old one (no lost or duplicated frames, user never appears offline), and reconciling is deferred one tick so React StrictMode's mount/unmount/mount doesn't churn connections. Non-Pulse listeners are never sent `pulse` frames. Verified in Playwright: landing on /positions gets 0 pulse frames; SPA-navigating to /pulse swaps to `?pulses=1` and frames flow (11 in 6 requests); navigating back swaps to the plain stream with 0 frames; 3 full swap rounds leave exactly one stream open and the server's `notificationStreamConnections` steady (System Health); a `pg_notify` `position_closed` still toasts after the swaps. `PulsePage.tsx` passes `{ wantsPulses: true }`.
- **Shared notification stream + "N/S" badge, 2026-09-19 (front end, uncommitted).** Prices "never arrived" because Chrome allows only 6 HTTP/1.1 connections per host across ALL tabs and each tab burned 4 on the Positions page (BackgroundJobsContext, PositionsPage, PulsePage and TickerDetailModal each opened their own copy of the identical `/notifications/stream`, plus greeks + P&L); two tabs starved every later stream. `openNotificationStream` in `src/api/notifications.ts` now ref-counts one shared EventSource per tab (no call-site changes): Positions 4→3 sockets, Ticker Detail modal +4→+3. Verified in Playwright: a page mount opens no second notification stream, a `pg_notify` `position_closed` showed the BackgroundJobs toast and a `position_opened` triggered exactly one PositionsPage refetch over the one connection, and all streams release on modal close. Only a partial fix — one Positions tab + an open modal is still 6 streams, so two heavy tabs can still starve; HTTP/2 removes the per-host cap, and **prod already has it** (checked 2026-09-19: the deployed front end's VITE_API_BASE_URL is https://api.ioriore.com, which is Cloudflare-proxied to the Heroku router and negotiates HTTP/2 — the bare herokuapp.com address is HTTP/1.1, which is a red herring, nobody uses it). So the 6-connection starvation is a **dev-only** problem (plain http://localhost:3030); production users are unaffected. Remaining dev-only options are parked at low priority — see "Live-stream connection cap in dev" under Optional / future ideas. Unrelated pre-existing bug seen while testing: closing Ticker Detail throws `Object is disposed` from lightweight-charts (chart painting after disposal; reproduced 8/8 on the original code). Also: `unstructured` positions' Positions-table badge is now **N/S** (`strategyAbbrev`; full label stays "Needs Review").
- **Expired covered call now explains its Needs Review leftover stock, 2026-09-19 (committed fa7f1e0, NOT yet deployed to the VPS worker).** When a covered call expires worthless, the position holding the stock is relabeled `unstructured` in place (never closed), so `determineLeftoverStockReason`'s "most recently closed covered_call/CSP" lookup could never match it — AAOI/NBIS sat at `unstructured_reason='unknown'` and logged an `unexplained_leftover_stock` anomaly every pass (18k+ rows since 8/29). It now first checks the ticker's open positions for a short call past expiry (ET) or already swept closed with no closing trade → `cc_expired_leftover_stock`. Reason is recomputed every pass, so live rows self-correct within a minute of deploy; historical closed `unknown` rows deliberately not backfilled. Verified by running the exact query read-only against prod (AAOI/NBIS match, MU control doesn't). Needs `npm run deploy:worker` (ask first) to take effect.

- **Trade Alerts screen now sorted by ticker, 2026-09-17.** Default `GET /trade-alerts` ordering (`src/routes/tradeAlerts.ts`) changed from "most-recently-generated ticker group first" to alphabetical by symbol (yield DESC still breaks ties within a ticker) — applies to both the New Trade Alerts and Roll Alerts sections, since they share one query and the frontend just renders rows in whatever order the API returns. Verified against real dev-DB data (54 approved + 3179 expired alerts across 20 symbols) via direct API call and live in the browser — both confirmed correct alphabetical grouping.
- **New-trade alerts suppressed for tickers already holding a position, 2026-09-15** (`src/ibkr/generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts`, `src/lib/positionQueries.ts`). Marcelo's complaint: the nightly scan kept suggesting brand-new covered-call/CSP trades on tickers where a position was already open — noise, since the roll scan already covers what to do about an existing position. New `fetchOpenPositionStrategyKeys(tickerId)` feeds `generateTradeAlertCandidatesForTicker`'s existing `effectiveSettingsByStrategy` logic: `cash_secured_put` new-trade candidates are suppressed outright whenever any open CSP position exists on that ticker (no partial-coverage concept for CSP, so any exposure means no room — deliberate trade-off, explicitly ruled out supporting put-laddering); `covered_call` only suppresses when an open covered-call position exists **and** there's no uncovered stock left to write a fresh contract against (uncovered shares < 100 sharesPerContract) — the existing "write against remaining naked shares" case (uncovered shares ≥ 100) is untouched. Applies to both the automated shortlist scan and the manual per-ticker "Scan/Refresh" button, since both share this function. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, existing `generateTradeAlertCandidates.test.ts` suite (8/8) still passes, and the new query manually run against the local dev DB confirmed correct output for a real open CSP position.
- **Pulse's Latest Events panel now populated on load, 2026-09-15** (both repos). Previously purely in-memory/live — `PulsePage.tsx`'s `events` state only ever grew from the open `/notifications/stream` SSE connection, so it always started as "No events yet." on a fresh page load, with no persistence anywhere for past `AppNotification`s. New `notification_events` table (migration `20260915000001`, capped to the most recent 200 rows via a delete-after-insert in `publishNotification`, since the panel only ever shows the latest 30) — every notification except `presence` (online/offline state, not a loggable event) is now persisted alongside the existing `pg_notify`. New `GET /notifications/recent` returns the most recent rows newest-first; the frontend's new `fetchRecentNotifications()` (`api/notifications.ts`) is called once on `PulsePage` mount, reusing the same text/color formatting as the live SSE handler (including the `order_status` case's `fetchOrder` lookup) but skipping the pulse-animation/side-effect refreshes that only make sense for events as they happen live; guarded against clobbering any events the live stream appends while the fetch is still in flight. Verified end-to-end: migration applied to local dev DB, `GET /notifications/recent` curled directly against a set of manually-inserted test rows (confirmed newest-first ordering and that a `presence` row is correctly excluded/filtered), a real `publishNotification()` call confirmed to persist via a one-off `tmp/` script, and the populated panel confirmed live in the browser via Playwright before the test rows were cleaned up. Both repos typecheck clean. **Follow-up same day**: order events now include a short per-leg summary — stock legs show `action qty @ price`, option legs show `action qty strike-right DTE @ premium` (`formatOrderLegsSummary`, shared by both the live and backfill code paths); multi-leg orders (e.g. a roll's close+open legs) join with " + ". DTE is computed relative to the event's own timestamp, not "today" — mirrors `daysToExpiry`'s existing `asOf` convention, so a backfilled historical event for a long-expired option shows the DTE it actually had then, not a confusing negative countdown. One landmine avoided: `OrderLeg.expiry` isn't consistently raw IBKR `YYYYMMDD` — a roll order's close-leg had it as a full ISO timestamp instead — handled with the same `length === 8` conditional already used in `OrderReviewPanel.tsx`/`TickerDetailModal.tsx`. Verified live via Playwright against real historical orders (single-leg CSP fills and a 2-leg AMAT roll) pulled from the dev DB.
- **Roll alert strikes missing from the option chain — found live 2026-09-15, quick-fixed same day, then given a long-term fix same day.** Marcelo flagged a real roll alert (AMAT, close $437.50C, replacement $435C) where both strikes were missing from the Sep 18 expiry's chain — visibly a gap between $432.50 and $440 in an otherwise clean $2.50 grid. Root cause: `fetchPendingAlertStrikesByExpiry`'s `mustIncludeStrikes` query only pulled `new_trade` alert strikes, explicitly excluding roll alerts (correct when written 2026-08-26, since roll alerts weren't shown in this modal at all then) — stale after the same-day roll-alert-into-instrument-view work above, since a roll's close/replacement strikes can now need to render directly in this chain. First fix: hand-added roll-alert strike extraction alongside the existing new_trade one. **Long-term fix, same day, after discussing the recurring pattern** (this is at least the second time a "this strike must be visible" case went missing from this query): the underlying problem isn't the specific missing case, it's that the must-include query has to hand-parse each alert type's JSON shape, so nothing forces a return trip to it when a new case starts needing chain visibility. New `trade_alerts.referenced_strikes` column (migration `20260915000002`, jsonb array of `{expiry, strike}`) populated by one shared pair of helpers (`referencedStrikesForNewTrade`/`referencedStrikesForRoll`, `src/lib/tradeAlertReferencedStrikes.ts`) at every insert/update site (`runTradeAlertGeneration.ts` ×2, `refreshTickerTradeAlerts.ts`, `refreshTradeAlert.ts` ×2) — the chain's must-include query now reads this one flat, type-agnostic column instead of parsing `suggested_structure` per alert_type, so a future alert shape can't repeat this class of bug. **Also fixed a related, previously-unnoticed gap**: a currently-*held* position's own option strike was only ever guaranteed visible by coincidence (when an alert happened to reference it) — now unconditionally unioned in from `position_legs` for every open option leg on that symbol, independent of alerts entirely. Verified live twice: a synthetic far-OTM roll alert's strikes (`$465`/`$467.50`) rendered correctly via the new column; a real position leg's strike, temporarily moved to a real future expiry with zero pending alerts, also rendered correctly via the new position-legs union. `tsc --noEmit` and full test suite (36/36) clean. Existing pending alerts predating this migration default to `referenced_strikes: []` — self-heals on their next refresh/regeneration, not backfilled.
- **Pulse chart relabeled "Today's P&L · live" → "Unrealised P&L", 2026-09-15** (`PulsePage.tsx`) — more accurate name for a live mark-to-market sum of currently-open positions since each one's own entry, distinct from the "Yesterday's P&L" KPI's day-over-day snapshot delta.
- **Roll alerts folded into the instrument view; covered-call quantity default fixed, 2026-09-15** (`iorio-reloaded-app`). Two items Marcelo raised the prior day, addressed same-session. (1) **Roll alerts**: acting on one no longer opens a separate stacked `RollPositionModal` — its setup UI (close/replacement leg live quotes, limit-price inputs, order-building) was extracted into `RollOrderSetupForm.tsx` and now renders inline in `TickerDetailModal`'s existing Order Setup slot, the same one chain-picks and new-trade alerts already use, ending in the same shared `OrderReviewPanel` confirm step. Roll alerts also now show in the modal's Trade Alerts table (own "Roll Alerts" sub-list, previously excluded entirely) alongside the Position card banner. Every "Roll" button app-wide — Positions table, Trade Alerts page, Position card — now opens `TickerDetailModal` (via a new `initialAlertId` pre-select path that also matches roll alerts, not just new-trade ones) instead of the old standalone modal, so every roll originates from the instrument view as asked. `RollPositionModal.tsx` deleted. (2) **Real bug fixed**: selecting a covered-call strike in the option chain always defaulted the Contracts input to 1, regardless of how many shares of that unstructured position were already held uncovered — 200 held shares defaulted to 1 contract instead of 2. `TickerDetailModal` now computes uncovered shares from open unstructured positions for the symbol and defaults the quantity to `floor(uncoveredShares / 100)`; the helper text under the input now states the actual held/covered share counts instead of a generic "netted out automatically" line. Verified live end-to-end in the dev app (markets open): a temporarily-reactivated test position (200 uncovered INTC shares) correctly defaulted to 2 contracts with the right note text; a manually-inserted test roll alert against a real AMAT position confirmed the whole Roll flow (Positions table click → instrument view opens focused on the position → banner/leg button → inline Roll Order Setup panel with live-streamed quotes and prefilled limit prices → Cancel clears cleanly) with zero console errors — all test data removed afterward. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` and `oxlint` clean.
- **Iorio Pulse follow-ups, 2026-09-19.** (1) Latest Events blanks: each row started at `opacity: 0` and only faded in after two `requestAnimationFrame` calls, which never fire in a backgrounded/occluded tab, leaving invisible rows; replaced with a CSS-only `@keyframes` entry animation (`PulsePage.css`). Cause inferred from code + the fact it didn't reproduce locally, not confirmed in prod. (2) `position_closed` events were missing from `notification_events`: the VPS worker running before its 2026-09-19 17:08 UTC restart predated the persistence code (added 2026-09-15; worker deploys are manual), so closes were published live but never saved; the current build has persistence. Not re-verified against a fresh expiry close yet. (3) "Attention Needed" pill now has a hover/focus/tap tooltip listing each active problem (gateway stale/disconnected with last-update age or status code, IBKR account-data error) — mockup approved, `AttentionPill` in `PulsePage.tsx`; touch devices gate on `(hover: hover)` because a tap fires hover+focus+click. (4) Market card lists every exchange across `tickers.primary_exchange` (was only exchanges with an open position, so it showed just NASDAQ); now BATS · NASDAQ · NYSE. (5) New Unrealised P&L KPI tile between Yesterday's P&L and Allocation: value = live sum of per-position `unrealizedPnl` (same as the chart); % = `unrealisedPnl / (accountValue - unrealisedPnl) * 100` (approved formula, mirrors Yesterday's P&L's pre-move baseline). KPI grid is now 3:3:3:3:8 (four tiles 15% each, Allocation 40%). (6) Position Deltas chart replaced by Profit Probability: plots the already-approved `probabilityByD2` (streamed on `/positions/greeks/stream`, no new formula) with a 0.5 threshold constant (`PROFIT_PROBABILITY_THRESHOLD`); chart component/file/CSS renamed to match (`ProfitProbabilityChart`); not yet seen with live data (needs market hours). (7) Database card: `Connections n / max` (total open, cap = role `rolconnlimit`, 20 on Essential-1), `DB size x / 10 GB` (limit from new required env var `DB_PLAN_MAX_SIZE_BYTES`, read lazily in `GET /system-health/db` only — deliberately NOT in shared `env.ts`, which would crash the VPS worker at boot), and Response time avg + Slowest (new `dbQueryTimingTracker.ts`: knex query events, rolling 5-min window, web-dyno queries only). Gateway card: Uptime now before Reconnects, "lifetime" suffix dropped (the counter is per-process and resets on worker restart). Green/amber/red on the Database + Gateway stats (approved cut-offs, constants at the top of `PulsePage.tsx`, helper `lib/statusThresholds.ts`): connections used 50/80%, DB size used 70/90%, response avg 50/200 ms, response max 500/2000 ms, reconnects 0 / 1-5 / >5, gateway uptime green ≥30 min else amber (no red; blank when disconnected). Only the connection count and the DB-size percent are coloured (not the "/ 100" or the MB). Gateway "In flight": green 0, amber 1-5, red >5 (was always amber). Other cards (Heroku, LLM, IBKR) not coloured yet. (8) Front End card shows last-active: new `users.last_seen_at` (migration `20260919000003`, runs in the release phase), stamped on every `/notifications/stream` connect AND disconnect (`userLastSeen.ts`); `GET /system-health/presence` now returns `{users:[{id,displayName,online,lastSeenAt}]}` — online first, then most recent, max 3, users with no activity and no open tab excluded (keeps the `genosuke_prod` service user out). Known gap: a Heroku dyno restart drops connections without a disconnect event, so a user's last-active can read as their connect time. Verified locally with temp users + a real tab close; offline styling verified, but "everyone offline" not seen because the dev server also had Marcelo's own tabs connected. **Prod deploy needs `DB_PLAN_MAX_SIZE_BYTES=10737418240` set on Heroku first** — without it `/system-health/db` 500s and, because Pulse fetches all health cards in one `Promise.all`, every system card goes blank. Verified in the dev app: tile matches the sum of the Positions panel P&Ls, % checked by hand, no tile wraps at 1440 (tiles start wrapping their delta below ~1200px wide). (9) Silent topology lines now animate: `heroku-browser` (every HTTP request), `heroku-db` (every knex query), `genosuke-llm` (each LLM call), `genosuke-db` (chat-history load/save), `ibkr-gateway` (worker: after each reqPositions reconciliation round trip, on placeOrder, on each orderStatus callback). New `{type:"pulse", edgeId}` notification: never persisted, never in Latest Events; web-dyno edges use in-process `emitPulse` (`pulseEmitter.ts`, throttled 0.5-1 s per edge, deliberately no Postgres round trip or the DB edge would pulse forever); the worker uses `publishPulse` over NOTIFY. Only the `/pulse` tab receives pulse frames (`/notifications/stream?pulses=1`). **`ibkr-gateway` only starts animating after `npm run deploy:worker`** (ibkrGatewayWorker.ts changed; NOT deployed — needs explicit approval). Pulse/glow duration for ALL lines is now 300 ms (was 1100). Also: Gateway and Database cards swapped grid positions (Gateway top row, Database bottom); P&L chart line is violet (`--pnl-line`). Header now shows the brand icon mark (`/brand/iorio-icon.svg`) left of "IORIO Pulse." (title-case Pulse). Allocation bar segments lighten (white overlay, 22%) on hover while their tooltip shows. **Open follow-ups for the 2026-09-19 Pulse batch (nothing below is confirmed yet):** (a) DONE 2026-09-19 ~18:51 UTC: worker deployed at `82d1754` via `npm run deploy:worker` (Marcelo added a permission rule for it; no orders in flight beforehand); service active, reconciliation #1 clean, no errors in the journal, `publishPulse` present in the build, 0 pulse rows persisted — the visual IBKR-Gateway dot in prod (about once a minute from reconciliation) not yet eyeballed; (b) confirm in prod that the next expiry/close writes a `position_closed` row to `notification_events` (the pre-17:08-UTC worker build lacked persistence; theory moderate-confidence); (c) Profit Probability chart and violet P&L line not seen with live data (need market hours); (d) `genosuke-llm` / `genosuke-db` pulses not exercised (need a Genosuke chat turn); (e) prod `users.last_seen_at` migration runs in the release phase on the next push; `DB_PLAN_MAX_SIZE_BYTES` is already set on Heroku (release v188); (f) Response-time row has only ~24px spare at 1706px wide, and the KPI-tile deltas wrap below ~1200px — shorten label / shrink font if either bites; (g) not coloured yet: Heroku, LLM, IBKR card stats. (h) Prod state at end of session: API v190 (`82d1754`) + frontend v96 (`a794186`) live on Heroku, worker on the VPS at `82d1754`; 0 server errors in the prod logs after deploy; `users.last_seen_at` migration applied. "too many connections for role" at 18:31 UTC 2026-09-19 — INVESTIGATED, root cause found, fix proposed and NOT built. Trigger: Pulse's Latest Events backfill (`fetchDescribedEvents` in `PulsePage.tsx`) fires one `GET /positions/orders/:id` per `order_status` event — unbounded, parallel, undeduped — on every page load AND every tab refocus (18 events that night ⇒ log bursts of 18/54/18/36 GETs at 18:16/18:25/18:26/18:31 UTC). Each authenticated request needs a session-store lookup (own pg pool, no `max`, default 10) plus a knex query (`connection.ts`, no pool config, default max 10), so a fan-out grows both web pools at once; with the LISTEN client (1), the worker's knex pool + LISTENs, and any scheduler job, the total passes Essential-1's role cap of 20 (`rolconnlimit`). Failure was a FATAL 53300 from `connect-pg-simple`; also exposed a latent bug: `errorHandler` in `app.ts` lacks a `response.headersSent` guard ("Cannot set headers after they are sent"). All burst GETs otherwise returned 304; no 5xx logged in the retained hour. Still live: every Pulse load/refocus repeats the burst until the 18 cancelled orders leave the newest 30 events. **All three fixes BUILT 2026-09-19 (pushed and deployed; prod Pulse reload not yet eyeballed after the deploy — expect 0 `/positions/orders/*` GETs in the router log per load):** (1) `/notifications/recent` now embeds each `order_status` event's order (status + payload) via ONE query (`fetchRecentNotificationEventsWithOrders`, missing orders come back `null`); the Pulse backfill no longer calls `fetchOrder` at all. Verified: 0 `/positions/orders/*` requests on load and on the refocus refetch, and the events still render ("Filled — NBIS: SELL 5 210P …") with no blanks. (2) Explicit pool ceilings in `src/config/databaseConnectionBudget.ts` (knex max 4 in `db/connection.ts`, session store max 3 in `middleware/session.ts`; budget web 8 + worker 6 + one job 4 = 18 of 20). Verified: 150 requests at 60-way concurrency all 200, peak 9 connections (= 8 web + my sampler). **Worker cap DEPLOYED 2026-09-19 ~19:14 UTC (`npm run deploy:worker` at `76d03a9`; no orders in flight; clean start, reconciliation #1 ok, no journal errors)** — API v193 + frontend v98 were live just before it; the budget is now true end to end. (Originally: the worker only got its cap after `npm run deploy:worker`) (it shares `connection.ts`), and scheduler jobs pick it up with the next web build; until then the worker still runs knex max 10, so the 18-of-20 budget is not yet true end to end. (3) `errorHandler` extracted to `middleware/errorHandler.ts` with a `headersSent` guard + 2 vitest cases (suite: 38 passing). Side effect to remember: knex queries beyond 4 concurrent now QUEUE instead of opening more connections, so a script that fans out many parallel DB calls is throttled, not failed. (i) `Bash(npm run deploy:worker)` is now an allowed permission rule on Marcelo's machine, so worker deploys no longer hit the classifier — still check for in-flight orders first and tell Marcelo before running. (j) Suggested next check: Monday 2026-09-21 US market hours — eyeball Profit Probability, the violet P&L line, the Unrealised P&L tile, DB response times and the IBKR-Gateway dot in prod. Verified locally: stream filter (0 vs 11 frames), browser/db/ibkr dots in the page (ibkr via a simulated NOTIFY), dot lifetime 337 ms, publishPulse persists nothing; genosuke-llm/-db not exercised (needs a Genosuke chat turn); violet checked via resolved token only (no unrealised P&L data locally with the market closed).
- **Iorio Pulse follow-up fixes, 2026-09-14.** Market-status chip now shows real exchange data instead of a hardcoded "NASDAQ · NYSE": `tickers.primary_exchange` (migration `20260914000001`) captured from IBKR's `Contract.primaryExch` at ticker-creation time (`fetchNewTickerData.ts`/`findOrCreateTicker.ts`), backfilled for all existing tickers via a one-off `tmp/backfillPrimaryExchange.ts` (run against prod DB from a local machine, since prod DB writes and `heroku run` are both blocked for Claude) — turned up that DRAM/LITX actually trade on BATS, not NASDAQ/NYSE. New `src/lib/marketSessionStatus.ts` computes a real 4-state pre-market/open/after-hours/closed session (green/amber/red) off a fixed US-equities schedule plus the existing `market_calendar` table for holiday awareness (no live IBKR polling needed, since NASDAQ/NYSE/BATS all share identical hours) — exposed via new `GET /system-health/market-status`. Also fixed a real bug: `.mk-status` in `PulsePage.css` was hardcoded green regardless of state, so "CLOSED" rendered in green text before this. Bundled in the same pass: P&L chart tooltip now shows local time alongside the value; Top Alerts panel deduped to one (highest-yield) alert per ticker instead of a flat top-5 (mirrors the Trades panel's "fetch generously, let `overflow:hidden` clip" pattern, no scroll); Database card shows a real `state='active'` connection count and dynamic MB/GB sizing (was always rounding to "0.0 GB" for a 26MB dev DB) instead of the meaningless total-connections/`max_connections` ratio, and dropped its unrelated "Open positions" row; IBKR card shows Total Cash/Reserved Cash (from the existing `cashLockedInCsps` figure, already computed for the Dashboard/Risk Limits screens but unused here) instead of Buying Power/Margin Excess/Cash; Frontend and LLM topology cards swapped grid positions; the "Update available" toast's Reload button now shows a spinner and disables itself; the topbar's "IORIO Pulse" pill switched from flex-remaining-space centering (which drifted off-center since the logo and icon-group widths differ) to true `left:50%` viewport centering.

- **Iorio Pulse shipped, 2026-09-13.** New real-time ops-room dashboard — a full-screen, chromeless, forced-dark 10th screen reached via a glowing "IORIO Pulse" link in the topbar (`AppLayout.tsx`, new pulsing-dot CSS in `theme.css`) that opens `/pulse` in a new tab, nested directly under `ProtectedRoute` but outside `AppLayout` so it skips the sidebar/topbar entirely (`App.tsx`). Design went through several rounds as an HTML/CSS mockup (artifact) before being ported to real React — see that conversation for the visual decisions. Shows: 4 KPI cards (Account Value, Available Cash, Today's P&L, an Allocation bar with a CSS-only hover tooltip — the first version relied on the native `title` attribute, which doesn't render reliably inside the artifact/iframe context it was prototyped in), a Positions panel and a "Top Alerts by yield" panel, a 7-node system topology map (IBKR, Gateway, Database, Genosuke, LLM, Heroku, Front End) connected by SVG edges with small colored circles that animate along a connector when a real backend event fires, two live-sampled charts (Total P&L, Position Deltas vs. the real configured `delta_target_max`), and Trades + System Events panels. Everything is real data reused from existing endpoints (`/positions`, `/dashboard/*`, `/risk-limits/exposure`, `/trade-blotter`) — no new aggregate endpoint was needed. New backend surface, all in `iorio-reloaded-api`: `src/lib/notificationChannel.ts`'s `AppNotification` union widened (`job_completed`/`alert_generated`/`genosuke_reply`/`presence`) with publish call sites added at `runJob.ts`, `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts` (batched once per ticker for `new_trade`, not per-row, to avoid flooding the event feed during a 60+-ticker nightly scan), and `genosuke/bot.ts`; `src/lib/presenceTracker.ts` (new, in-memory) piggybacks on the existing `/notifications/stream` SSE connection every authenticated page already opens, so "online" means "using the app at all," not "has Pulse open" (explicit tradeoff); `src/genosuke/llmStats.ts` (new) times every OpenRouter call for the LLM node's calls/min and avg latency; `fetchAccountSummary.ts` gained a new `ExcessLiquidity` tag (additive, every existing caller unaffected) for the IBKR node's real margin-excess figure; a new `worker_health` table (migration `20260913000001`) is upserted every 45s by `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` and read by a new `GET /system-health/gateway` — deliberately a table the worker writes and the web dyno reads, not a `pg_notify` event, since this is state ("what's the connection's status right now") not an event, and piggybacking onto the notification channel would mean a fresh web-dyno restart reads "unknown" until the next heartbeat. New `GET /system-health/{presence,db,genosuke,web-dyno}` routes, `GET /trade-alerts?sort=yield&limit=N`, and a small `requestRateTracker.ts` middleware for Heroku's real request rate. **Explicitly not built, by design**: persisted intraday P&L/delta history (both charts sample the already-live SSE streams client-side instead — start empty on page load, fill in as the tab stays open, no new snapshot job/table/retention decision taken on); "Scheduler: next run" (no free real source without a Heroku Platform API token) and "Streams active" on the Gateway node (doesn't correspond to anything the worker tracks) were dropped rather than faked; Genosuke reply events carry no user attribution (chat-level-only auth, confirmed no way to know which of Marce/Juan triggered a given exchange). One real bug caught during live browser testing (not before): two of the dev DB's real open positions share the ticker "SPCX" (a rolled position, two distinct rows) — the delta chart was originally keyed by symbol, which collided into a duplicate React key and a divide-by-zero NaN in the SVG polyline; fixed by keying on position id throughout `PulsePage.tsx`/`PositionDeltasChart.tsx`. Verified end-to-end live in the actual dev app (not just curl): logged in, clicked the nav link, confirmed a genuine new tab with no chrome, confirmed real numbers matching the Dashboard exactly, ran a real job through `runJob()` and watched it animate + log in System Events with zero console errors, confirmed the Allocation tooltip and no-scroll claim (`scrollHeight === innerHeight` at 1440×900) directly in the browser.
- **Iorio Pulse — round of live production feedback addressed, 2026-09-14 (`iorio-reloaded-app` only).** After Marcelo pushed 2026-09-13's build himself and used it live at `app.ioriore.com/pulse`, he sent 9 pieces of feedback. **Investigated first, not a code bug**: production's Gateway node showed "not connected" while the real worker was healthy — confirmed via VPS SSH that `iorio-worker.service` has been running fine since well before the `worker_health`-writing code shipped, so it's simply deployed code that predates that table and has never written to it. Real fix is a VPS worker deploy, deliberately not done as part of this pass — flagged separately since it needs explicit sign-off per the standing `ibkrGateway*`/worker-deploy rule. **Everything else implemented**: Trades and Latest Events (renamed from "System Events") panels raised from 6/17-row caps to 30 each, relying on the panels' existing `overflow:hidden` to clip without a scrollbar, per "as much as you can fit, no scrolling"; both `TotalPnlChart` and `PositionDeltasChart` gained a hover crosshair + tooltip (`.chart-tooltip`/`.chart-tooltip-multi` in `PulsePage.css`) showing the exact P&L or per-position delta value under the cursor; the delta chart is now filtered to `covered_call`/`cash_secured_put` positions only, both in the rolling-sample loop and the series passed to the chart; the Allocation bar gained a 4th "Unstructured" segment (`strategyPct("unstructured")` — a real backend strategy key, confirmed via `PositionStrategyKey`, renders at genuine 0% width when no unstructured positions are open, which is why it wasn't visible in the screenshots taken this round); `FlashingNumber` (extended with an optional `style` prop, additive) is now wired across every genuinely-ticking numeric display — KPI strip, positions table, all topology node sub-metrics — deliberately excluding the two dyno/gateway "Uptime" fields, which would flash every 30s poll on a monotonically-increasing number and add noise, not signal; the "Today's P&L" KPI tile was renamed "Yesterday's P&L" (it's the day-over-day snapshot delta) and the "Total P&L · live" chart renamed "Today's P&L · live" (it's the live mark-to-market sum) with both now colored red/green by sign instead of a fixed color; the status pill is now real and computed, not static text — `systemDegraded = gatewayDown || ibkrDataError`, where `gatewayDown` is `worker_health` being stale/missing or reporting `connected:false`, and `ibkrDataError` is `GET /risk-limits/exposure`'s `accountDataError` being set — showing "ATTENTION NEEDED" (red) or "ALL SYSTEMS NOMINAL" otherwise. **One self-caught bug**: the P&L rename left a stale internal variable name (`todaysPnl`) and an explanatory comment still describing the old KPI/chart labels; renamed to `yesterdaysPnl` throughout and updated the comment before it shipped — caught by a leftover-reference console error during Playwright verification (traced to Vite HMR serving a stale module, confirmed the saved file was already correct, fixed by a hard navigation). Verified live in the dev app: two full chart-sampling cycles (75s × 2) waited out to get real rendered lines, both hover tooltips screenshotted showing correct values against the visible chart state, status pill confirmed correctly red against local dev's genuinely-disconnected Gateway, `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` and `oxlint` clean, zero console errors post-fix. Not yet pushed (commit pending Marcelo's review).

- **Recurring HOOD reconciliation discrepancy root-caused and fixed, 2026-09-11.** Position reconciliation kept alerting a local/IBKR quantity mismatch on HOOD stock, previously assumed already fixed by the 2026-09-10 AMAT/SPCX split-covered-call fix. Traced via worker logs (`journalctl -u iorio-worker`) to a distinct case: IBKR filled a call roll lot-by-lot (three separate 1-lot buy-back/sell-open pairs over ~4s) instead of atomically. Mid-roll, `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` correctly shrinks the old covered call's stock leg and grows the new one's proportionally on each pass — but once the old call's *last* lot clears and its contract fully disappears from IBKR's held positions, that old position is never revisited again (the per-symbol loop only processes currently-held short calls), freezing its stock leg at whatever partial quantity it last held instead of zeroing it. The general closing pass doesn't catch it either, since it only checks whether a contract ID is held *at all* — HOOD stock is still held, just entirely reassigned to the new position by then. Net effect: a stale, permanently double-counted stock leg. Fixed in `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` (`src/ibkrGatewayWorker.ts:1192-1226`) — the orphan-stock-leg check now runs every pass regardless of whether this position already owns a stock leg, and closes (rather than silently skips) a stale candidate once its sibling option is confirmed no longer held. Deployed to the VPS worker; the one live stranded leg (`position_legs` id `8eaa9229`) closed manually via SQL since prod DB writes are otherwise blocked by policy. Verified: reconciliation alert cleared post-fix.
- **Negative Day Residual investigated — two real bugs found and fixed 2026-09-11, one structural noise source identified and not fixed.** Marcelo asked why the "P&L by Period" table's Residual bucket showed -$3,982 for Day on 2026-09-10, expecting it to reflect dividends/interest. Ruled that out first — IBKR's Flex Query showed zero cash transactions (no interest, dividends, fees, transfers) in the prior 7 days. **Bug 1, fixed**: `computeStrategyPeriodPnl`/`computeStrategyDailyPnlSeries` (`src/lib/strategyPeriodPnl.ts`) attributed a closed position's *entire lifetime* realized P&L to the period it closed in, instead of just the P&L accrued since that period's start — a multi-day trade closing today dumped its whole history into today's "Day" figure, double-counting what earlier days' unrealized deltas had already reported. Confirmed via MU's cash-secured put: +$1,541 already-recognized unrealized P&L as of the prior close, then its full +$1,542 lifetime gain got attributed entirely to the closing day. Fixed by netting each closed position's lifetime P&L against its own `position_pnl_snapshots` baseline as of the period start, mirrored in both functions (~$445 of the gap). **Bug 2, root-caused, already fixed by an earlier same-day commit (`402dc39`, deployed after 9/10)**: the nightly snapshot job silently skips writing a `position_pnl_snapshots` row for an entire position if even one leg's price fetch fails — confirmed via `job_runs.details` (`{"skipped": 2}` for 9/10) cross-referenced against missing snapshot rows for MU and a freshly-reopened AMAT covered call. Their real day's market move never landed in any strategy bucket, leaking straight into Residual — not reconstructable after the fact, since the underlying price data was never captured. **Structural finding, addressed by a timing fix, not fully eliminable**: even on days with no missing snapshots, `account_pnl_snapshots.unrealized_pnl` (IBKR's own ledger figure) vs. `SUM(position_pnl_snapshots.unrealized_pnl)` (this platform's own tracked total) diverges by hundreds to thousands of dollars with no stable sign or offset (checked Aug 25 – Sep 10: -$1,940 to +$3,074). Ruled out local cost-basis drift (`position_legs.entry_price` mirrors IBKR's own `avgCost` directly, re-synced every reconciliation pass — `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts:998-1015`). Root cause: `run-daily-pnl-snapshot-job.ts` captured account-level net-liq, then ran Flex cash-flow reconciliation (up to 120s of IBKR polling), *then* captured per-position prices — real market moves during that gap on volatile/thin names (MU, SMCI, SPCX, COIN) show up as Residual noise, independent of any bug. **Fixed**: reordered the job so `fetchAccountSummary`/`fetchAccountLedgerPnl`/`fetchLivePrices` all launch together via `Promise.allSettled` (each still fails independently, same isolation as before), with `reconcileCashFlows` moved to run after both snapshots are written. Residual doesn't need to match IBKR's own ledger number — it only needs the two halves of the job's own math to reflect the same moment in the market. Verified end-to-end against the local dev DB (live paper account via VPS tunnel): 8/8 open positions snapshotted, 0 skipped, `job_runs` recorded success; test rows cleaned up after. Three commits: `4486368` (realized double-counting fix), `0031ba6` (capture timing fix); bug 2's fix (`402dc39`) predates this investigation. Not yet pushed.
- **Roll alert surfaced on Positions screen + Ticker Detail, shipped 2026-09-10** (`iorio-reloaded-app`, frontend only — the roll-alert data model and roll execution flow already existed). Positions table now shows a `Roll` button next to `Close` only when a position has a real pending roll alert (`PositionsPage.tsx`, fetches `GET /trade-alerts?status=pending` alongside positions, matched by `relatedPositionId`); clicking opens the existing `RollPositionModal` pre-filled from the real alert instead of the on-demand roll-candidate path. Ticker Detail's Position card (`PositionCard.tsx`) now shows a warning banner with the alert's rationale plus a "Review Roll" button when its position has a pending roll alert, and its leg's manual "Roll" button is replaced by a solid "Roll Alert" button for the matching leg — both open the same modal off the real alert. `/ui-ux` review caught one real issue (the banner's action button used `btn-warning` sitting on an `alert-warning` background — a colour clash) fixed to `btn-outline-secondary`. Verified live via Playwright on both screens (desktop + mobile) by temporarily reactivating a real expired dev-DB roll alert, then restoring it.
- **IBKR read-latency investigation, continued — root cause found, FROZEN-then-live SSE architecture built for greeks/pnl/portfolio/exposure, 2026-09-10. Backend only, frontend not started.** Follow-up to 2026-09-09's entry below, whose open item ("Gateway degraded, check health") turned out to be a red herring — Marcelo pushed back (rightly) that IBKR load had already been ruled out in prior sessions, so the real mechanism needed finding, not more waiting. Ran the built-in health check (`POST /system-health/check-ibkr`) — genuinely healthy, `competingLiveSession: false`, ruling out the one specific documented failure mode that check exists for. **Real root cause found empirically** (`tmp/testFrozenMarketData.ts`): IBKR's `REALTIME` market data type is unreliable for options specifically — a snapshot can sit with zero ticks for 8+ seconds even in complete isolation. `FROZEN` market data type (last known good data, not gated on a live trade occurring) returned complete price+delta for all 4 real open option legs in ~2.4-2.8s, every time. Since FROZEN isn't genuinely live (would show stale prices during real market hours if used alone), the approved design is: show FROZEN immediately, then silently upgrade to `REALTIME` in the background and update only once real data arrives — matching this app's existing SSE/live-quote conventions (`streamTickerDetail.ts`, `streamOrderLegQuote.ts`, the `useFlashOnChange` flash-on-change UI pattern) rather than inventing a new one. **Built**: `streamLivePrices`/`streamLiveGreeks` (new exports alongside the existing one-shot `fetchLivePrices`/`fetchLiveGreeks`, `src/ibkr/`) — FROZEN snapshot phase, then a genuine REALTIME streaming subscription kept open until the caller aborts, emitting again only on a real change. `streamPositionExposures` (`src/lib/positionExposure.ts`) — same shape, reusing the exposure math as a pure function of whatever prices are currently known (a leg with no live price yet already fell back to `entry_price`, so this needed no extra regression-guarding, unlike greeks/pnl below). New SSE routes: `GET /positions/greeks/stream`, `/positions/pnl/stream`, `/dashboard/portfolio/stream`, `/risk-limits/exposure/stream` — each mirrors its one-shot sibling's query parsing, math, and snapshot-fallback exactly, applying the fallback only to the first (frozen) event. **Two more real bugs found and fixed, both live-tested, not assumed**: (1) IBKR doesn't necessarily send all 4 greeks fields on every `tickOptionComputation` tick — a tick carrying only theta was wiping a previously-good delta/gamma/vega back to null in both the one-shot `fetchLiveGreeks` and the new streaming version; fixed by merging each tick into the previous reading instead of replacing it wholesale. (2) A race condition in every SSE route: the `onUpdate` callback does async work (a DB fallback query) on its first call, but `streamLiveGreeks`/`streamLivePrices` invoke it synchronously without awaiting — so a later, still-incomplete update could race ahead and reach the client before an earlier, more-complete one finished, showing as a value flickering back to null. Fixed with a new general-purpose `src/lib/serializeAsyncCalls.ts`, applied to all four streams — forces every update to fully finish (send included) before the next one starts. **Verified**: all four streams tested directly via curl — FROZEN/fallback values show within ~3s, genuinely live updates arrive afterward, confirmed zero regressions across repeated runs post-fix (was flapping real→null→real before). **Not yet done**: frontend — `EventSource` consumers in `api/positions.ts`/`api/dashboard.ts`/`api/riskLimits.ts`, and wiring `PositionsPage.tsx`/`DashboardPage.tsx`/`RiskLimitsPage.tsx` to consume the streams instead of their current one-shot fetches, with flash-on-change on update. The old one-shot `/greeks`, `/pnl`, `/portfolio`, `/exposure` GET routes are untouched and still work exactly as before — nothing currently depends on the new streams yet, so this commit changes no visible behavior. Diagnostic scripts (`tmp/testFrozenMarketData.ts`, `tmp/testStreamLiveGreeksRaw.ts`, others) gitignored, not committed.
- **IBKR read-latency investigation — several real bugs found and fixed 2026-09-09, one root cause still open.** Benchmarked every endpoint the UI calls; everything DB-only was 1-30ms, but 8 endpoints touching live IBKR data took 4.5-9.9s. Root cause: each opened a brand-new SSH tunnel + IBKR connection per request (`connectIbkr.ts`'s one-shot pattern), paying a ~4.5s connect tax before any data was even requested. Design proposed and approved (artifact "Shared IBKR Read Connection") to reuse one connection instead; built `src/ibkr/sharedReadConnection.ts` (lazy-connect, reconnect-with-backoff mirroring `ibkrGatewayPersistentConnection.ts`'s shape independently rather than refactoring that worker-critical file, shared `reqId` allocator, borrow/release with a fallback to the old one-shot path if unavailable) and migrated `fetchAccountSummary`, `fetchLiveGreeks`, `fetchLivePrices`, `searchTickers`, and `priceBarCache.ts`'s chart/iv-chart fetchers onto it. Also fixed, independently: (1) `priceBarCache.ts` was opening its IBKR connection *before* checking whether the chart cache was even stale, so a guaranteed cache hit still paid the full connect cost — now checks first (repeat chart views went from 4.6s to ~5ms). (2) `fetchLiveGreeks`/`fetchLivePrices` used to sleep a flat 5s regardless of how fast IBKR answered — now resolve as soon as data arrives. **Real regression caught and fixed same session**: moving `fetchAccountSummary` onto the shared connection exposed the exact bug [[project_eventemitter_once_concurrent_listeners]] already warns about — its `ib.once(accountSummaryEnd, ...)` self-removed on *any* concurrent call's completion, not just its own, so two Dashboard cards loading together (`/available-cash` + `/portfolio`) silently orphaned one listener and the second card timed out showing "–". Fixed to `.on()` with explicit per-call cleanup. **Second real bug found**: `/dashboard/portfolio` and `/risk-limits/exposure` both call the same zero-argument `computePositionExposures()`/`fetchAccountSummary()` whenever loaded together (always, on the Dashboard) — doubling concurrent IBKR work for identical data. New `src/lib/dedupeInFlight.ts` wraps both so concurrent callers share one in-flight call deterministically (not a stale-risk cache — once it resolves, the next call is fresh). Verified via a concurrent-request benchmark: both endpoints now complete within milliseconds of each other, confirming they share work. **Third real bug found, root cause, not yet proven to fully resolve the live symptom**: switched `fetchLiveGreeks`/`fetchLivePrices` to `reqMktData` snapshot mode (IBKR signals completion via `tickSnapshotEnd` instead of guessing with a timer) after measuring streaming mode's fixed-wait was both slow *and* incomplete on batches with 2+ contracts. Isolated testing (`tmp/testPortfolioContractsIsolated.ts`) then found `tickSnapshotEnd` never fires for an *option's* last-price snapshot specifically (stocks complete cleanly; all 4 tested option legs got a real price tick but no completion signal, even alone on a fresh connection) — fixed by resolving on the data arriving OR `tickSnapshotEnd`, whichever comes first, in both `fetchLivePrices.ts` and `fetchLiveGreeks.ts`. **Still open**: after this fix, Portfolio/Allocation were still slow/erroring on reload. Follow-up isolated tests (`tmp/testOptionTickTiming.ts`, `tmp/testOptionModelPrice.ts`) showed the exact same 4 option legs that returned clean data earlier in the session later returned *nothing at all* — no price, no greeks, no completion signal — on a brand-new, fully isolated connection with zero code-side contention. That points at the IBKR Gateway or its market-data farm connection itself being degraded (plausibly from this session's own heavy test volume), not a further code fix — **recommend checking Gateway health (`/system-health/check-ibkr` or the existing every-10-min job) before investigating further**, not yet done. Separately, noted but not a bug: DRAM (a shortlisted, recently-listed ETF) consistently fails to complete its snapshot with IBKR error 10089 ("requires additional subscription") — matches the already-tracked "Market data subscription blocked on funding threshold" open item, not new. **Verified fast and reliable, repeatedly, including in Marcelo's own browser**: `/dashboard/available-cash` (~1s, was 5s), `/shortlist/search` (~1s, was 5.3s), Ticker Detail chart/iv-chart (~5ms repeat view, was 4.6-6.2s every time). Diagnostic scripts left in `tmp/` (gitignored). Not yet deployed — local only.
- **reqHistoricalData hang recurrence — investigated 2026-09-09, prior root-cause theory disproven, two real gaps fixed and deployed.** — Marcelo flagged the health check's "silently hung" restart notification recurring for weeks. Pulled every occurrence since… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Trade Alerts: 24h auto-expiry + recency sort, shipped 2026-09-09** (`src/routes/tradeAlerts.ts`). Pending alerts now drop off the "Pending" list 24h after `created_at` (query-time filter only — no DB write, `status` stays `'pending'`, the existing `'expired'` enum value is left meaning "superseded by rescan" as before). Ticker groups on the list are now ordered by each ticker's most recent alert (`MAX(created_at) OVER (PARTITION BY ticker_id) DESC`) instead of alphabetically by symbol, with yield staying as the secondary sort within a ticker; no frontend change needed since `TradeAlertsPage.tsx` already renders groups in server order. Verified live via Playwright: the screen went from 19 ticker groups to the 3 actually inside the 24h window (PLTR/HOOD/AAOI), correctly ordered newest-first.
- **MU showing "expired" while still open, investigated and fixed 2026-09-09** (both repos). Marcelo flagged a screenshot: MU's Expiry column said "expired" but the position was still listed as open. Two independent, unrelated causes found. (1) **Display bug**: `daysToExpiry()`'s default `asOf` compares an expiry date parsed as UTC midnight against the viewer's local wall-clock time — on this WITA (UTC+8) machine, a same-US-trading-day expiry could show "expired" hours before the market had even closed. Fixed by anchoring the Positions/Calendar "today"/"expired" labels to a new `todayInEasternIso()` helper instead (`iorio-reloaded-app/src/lib/formatters.ts`) — `daysToExpiry`'s own default is left untouched since it also feeds `OrderReviewPanel`'s live DTE→POP calculation, a financial-formula change out of scope here. (2) **Real backend bug**: MU's "one position" was actually two unrelated cash-secured puts (different strikes, different expiries, opened 3 days apart) merged together — `runReconciliationPass` grouped short puts by symbol only, the exact bug already found and fixed for covered calls on 2026-08-25 (`upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition`) but never ported to CSPs. Fixed with a new `upsertSplitCashSecuredPutPosition` (`ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`) mirroring the covered-call fix, including the same one-time split-migration for pre-existing merged positions. **Follow-up bug found while verifying the split migration**: the newly split-off position's `opened_at` defaulted to `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` instead of carrying over the leg's real `entry_at` — a position opened days ago showed "Opened: today" right after being split. Fixed in the same function (and the covered-call one, same latent bug there since 2026-08-25). Verified end-to-end: frontend fix confirmed live via Playwright (MU showed "today" not "expired"); worker fix deployed to the VPS and confirmed via direct prod DB query that MU's two put legs now live in separate `positions` rows with correct strategy/status; local dev DB re-synced from prod to confirm the split shows correctly in the UI too (two separate MU rows, correct per-leg Structure/EXP $/Expiry). **`opened_at` fix**: deployed to the VPS (worker HEAD `33b9285` includes `7cf7267`, verified 2026-09-18), and MU's split prod position (`2c73dc3a-...`) now has the correct `opened_at` of `2026-09-03 17:09:27.725395+00` (verified via prod SELECT 2026-09-18).

- **P1: worker silently stopped processing orders for 3 days (Sep 5-8), root-caused, fixed, verified live in prod 2026-09-08.** — Marcelo flagged several orders stuck at "Confirmed — sending to IBKR..." for 42-58+ minutes. Root cause: at 11:02 UTC Sep 5, the… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
**Follow-up fix, same session: `SESSION_SECRET` moved out of shared `env.ts` into `middleware/session.ts` (commit `97b49b4`), deployed to both Heroku (`v134`) and the VPS worker.** The shared `config/env.ts` object was validating every env var eagerly at import for both consumers (web dyno + worker) even though `SESSION_SECRET` is only meaningful to the web dyno's express-session middleware — exactly what caused today's second deploy hiccup. Now validated lazily inside `session.ts` itself via the existing `requireEnvironmentVariable` helper, so the worker no longer references it at all (the placeholder left in the VPS `.env` from the unblock above is now just inert, safe to leave or remove). Verified: both repos typecheck/build clean, both processes redeployed and confirmed healthy (`heroku ps` → `web.1: up`; VPS journalctl → `active (running)`, LISTEN connected, no crash).

Also corrected a stale doc found along the way: `ibkr_health_check`'s Scheduler frequency was still documented as hourly in a few places here — it's been every 10 minutes since 2026-08-29 (re-confirmed via a 2026-09-08 Scheduler dashboard screenshot).

- **Positions table's Premium P&L/Stock P&L/Delta/Gamma blanking outside market hours — fixed 2026-09-08.** Root cause: both `/positions/pnl`'s snapshot fallback and `/positions/greeks` hard-coded `null` for these four fields whenever live IBKR pricing/greeks were unavailable (e.g. markets closed) — no persisted split/greeks existed to fall back to. Fixed with two additive migrations (`premium_pnl`/`stock_pnl` columns on `position_pnl_snapshots`; new `position_leg_greeks_snapshots` table), the nightly `run-daily-pnl-snapshot-job.ts` now also captures the premium/stock split and per-leg greeks (piggybacking on its existing one nightly IBKR round-trip), and both routes fall back to the latest snapshot row per field, each showing an "as of &lt;date&gt;" tooltip (Delta/Gamma didn't have this affordance before). Verified: migrations applied and round-tripped directly against dev DB; Delta/Gamma confirmed live in the browser now populating from tonight's snapshot. **Not yet verified**: Premium/Stock P&L display, since tonight's `position_pnl_snapshots` row for the 3 open positions was captured by prod's job *before* this migration existed (synced via `sync-dev-db`), so it has no split to show — confirm after tomorrow night's job run post-deploy. **Also found and fixed same session**: `formatDate()` (`iorio-reloaded-app` `src/lib/formatters.ts`) parsed a plain `YYYY-MM-DD` string as UTC midnight and formatted in the browser's local zone with no explicit `timeZone`, so any "as of" date badge (P&L %, and now Delta/Gamma) could show one day earlier than the actual snapshot date in a timezone behind UTC — reproduced live (DB row dated 2026-09-08, UI showed "As of Sep 7, 2026"). Fixed by parsing a plain-date string via the local `Date(year, month, day)` constructor instead of the UTC-instant path; verified live (now correctly shows "As of Sep 8, 2026"). A real timestamp (`formatDateTime`, or any full ISO string) is unaffected — only bare calendar-date strings hit the new path.

New `AlertCandidate.bidAskSpreadPct` (`(ask-bid)/premium×100`) and `trendLabel` (`"uptrend"|"downtrend"|"mixed"|null` from spot vs daily MA25 vs MA99, reusing `technicalIndicators.ts`'s existing math via a new `fetchTickerTrendLabel`/`buildTrendLabel` in `generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts`) — neither field touches `rankCandidates`' sort. `refreshTradeAlert.ts` recomputes spread fresh, carries `trendLabel` forward unchanged (same treatment as `technicalNote`/`ivRank`). Frontend: new `Spread`/`Trend` columns on Trade Alerts' New Trade and Roll tables (desktop + mobile), plus a real pre-existing bug fixed along the way — the Roll Alerts mobile card's POP field had no null-guard (`formatPercentage(replacement.probabilityOfProfit)` called directly), unlike every other POP render site; fixed to match. **Verified**: both repos typecheck clean, backend unit tests pass 8/8, live in local dev (light+dark) — new columns render correctly, and pre-existing pending alerts (predating these fields, `undefined` not `null` in their stored JSON) confirmed to degrade to a clean "—" rather than breaking (`TrendBadge` guards both). **Not yet verified**: real non-null values against a live IBKR quote — attempted via a live per-ticker refresh, got a 502 before reaching this code at all, consistent with the well-documented weekend/no-market-hours option-greeks gap elsewhere in this doc, not a regression from this change. Confirm next time markets are open (a real "Run Alerts Now" scan or per-ticker refresh should populate both fields on fresh candidates) — also worth a `/ui-ux` pass on the new columns once real values are on screen to check truncation/wrapping at mobile widths with populated (not dash) content.
