## Ticker Detail on a shared live connection (2026-09-19)
`streamTickerDetail` now borrows `sharedLiveConnection` (one-shot fallback kept): a second shared connection whose market data type is set to REALTIME once at connect and never changed (`requestRealtimeMarketData` is a no-op for it, via `markMarketDataTypeManaged`). All reqIds on shared connections come from that connection's counter through `nextReqIdFor(ib, fallback)` (fetchOptionChain lookups/quotes, priceBarCache's derived IV-bar id which used `reqId + 1000`, and the four fixed ids in streamTickerDetail). Fixed `ib.once()` misuse in `lookupPricingSnapshot` (`tickSnapshotEnd`) and `lookupContractDetails` (`contractDetailsEnd`); `lookupPricingSnapshot` now also cleans up its listeners and cancels its subscription on timeout.
- **Tested (Saturday, market closed):** solo vs 3 tickers concurrent (AAOI/NBIS/SPCX): identical prices, contract sets, chart bars, technicals; the one quote difference was a genuine late tick on the correct contract (SPCX 2026-09-25 152.5C, delta matches that position's leg). Listener counts on the live connection return to the idle baseline within 20s after full sessions and after disconnects at 2s/4s/7s. One-shot fallback verified by requesting before the live connection was ready. First data ~0.6s once warm (the connect used to come first); option chain ~10s (unchanged work: strike checks + quotes).
- **Known limitation:** if the shared live connection drops mid-session, open modals stop updating until reopened (same as a dropped one-shot connection); not handled.
- **Migration completed, verified 2026-09-25.** `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot.ts`/`fetchTickerOverview.ts`/`refreshTradeAlert.ts`/`evaluateRollForPosition.ts` now call `borrowSharedConnectionOrConnect` with `nextReqIdFor`'s per-connection allocator; `streamOrderLegQuote.ts` was rewritten onto `marketDataPool.ts`; `streamPositionQuote.ts` was deleted outright in the 2026-09-24 audit (`a6e168a`), confirmed zero remaining references repo-wide.

## Dashboard Portfolio-card load time (2026-09-19)
Measured, not guessed: the exposure SSE stream took ~9s to first data (account+CSP query ~1.0s, DB 4ms, new SSH tunnel ~3.7s + IBKR handshake ~1.7s per stream, FROZEN price ticks ~2.1s). Not a market-hours effect. Fixes: `streamLivePrices` and `streamLiveGreeks` now borrow the shared read connection (one-shot fallback kept); the exposure route starts the account fetch in parallel with the stream; `server.ts` warms the shared connection at startup. Result: Portfolio card ~2.6s in the browser (desktop + mobile checked), first request after a restart ~2.3s, greeks stream first data ~1.6s.
- **Migration completed, verified 2026-09-25** — see the same note above; `streamTickerDetail` was migrated separately (see "Ticker Detail on a shared live connection" above). `checkIbkrHealthJob` stays one-shot by design.
- **clientIds (changed later 2026-09-19):** the shared connections no longer use a fixed id. Each connect attempt draws a random id from its own range (read 1,000,000-1,499,999; live 1,500,000-1,999,999), above the one-shot connections (0-999,999) and the worker's fixed 42. A fixed 44 had let two processes booting together (duplicate dev servers) hang each other for 18.7s.

## Outage alert throttling (2026-09-19, built and deployed, verified in a real outage)
IBKR maintenance on a Saturday produced a Telegram message per systemd restart (worker) and per 10-min run (health check). Fix: state-based alerting. New `alert_state` table (row present = already alerted) and `src/lib/throttledAlert.ts` (`notifyDownThrottled`, `clearDownState`; atomic, so concurrent callers can't double-send). (A) `runJob` takes `failureAlertReminderIntervalMs`; `ibkr_health_check` uses 1h: first failure alerts, same-message repeats are muted, a changed message alerts immediately, recovery uses the existing streak message. (B) `persistentIbkrConnection.start()` no longer rejects on a failed connect; the worker stays up and retries with the existing backoff, so systemd stops restart-looping. The worker alerts once after 5 min disconnected (was 10; lowered 2026-09-19), reminds hourly, and sends one recovery message. Tested locally: throttle sequence (10 cases incl. concurrency), `start()` resolving with an unreachable tunnel, `runJob` 3 failures = 1 alert + recovery + state cleared. **Not tested:** a real Gateway outage end to end. **Deploy order:** GitHub push first (release phase creates `alert_state`), then `npm run deploy:worker` (manual, VPS). Other worker startup failures (DB down, etc.) still crash-and-alert per restart. **Bug found live and fixed (8e378b1; reached the VPS at 17:08 UTC. A 16:53 redeploy shipped nothing new because `deploy:worker` deploys origin/main and the fix wasn't pushed yet, so push first and verify the VPS file):** the worker's disconnect message embedded the elapsed time, and the throttle treats a changed message as a new alert, so it re-alerted every minute. Throttled messages must be constant text; reminders add the duration themselves. The redeploy causes one extra alert (stored text differs), then hourly. **Verified live 2026-09-19 (real IBKR outage, Gateway back at 17:24 UTC):** worker sent exactly 1 alert (17:19), no repeats, state cleared on reconnect; health check sent 1 alert at 16:41 and stayed quiet. 5-min threshold deployed 17:29 UTC (VPS at 2e0ffad, dist file checked). **Deploy lesson:** `deploy:worker` ships origin/main, not the local tree; push first, read the script's stderr warning, and grep the deployed dist on the VPS before calling it done.

## Stream multiplexing (built)
The design plan (D1–D5) that used to be here was implemented: git commit `4341611` "Stream multiplexer (always on), Price Performance redesign, strategy badge standardisation". The original plan, findings and decision log are in `PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md`.

## IBKR Gateway restart: false-alarm recovery + Pulse going stale on a restart (fixed 2026-09-23)
Two related bugs found from a real Telegram alert (`ibkr_health_check failed: ... restart didn't recover it`). **1) The alert itself was a false alarm, recurring:** `/opt/ibkr/restart-gateway.sh` on the VPS used a flat `sleep 25` after restarting the Gateway container, sized off one 2026-08-17 observation — not always enough (this run's logs show login not completing until ~30s), so the health check's reprobe sometimes hit a Gateway still mid-login and reported a false failure. `job_runs` showed this same pattern recurring at least 4 times in the prior two weeks, always self-clearing on the next 10-min check. **Fixed**: the script now polls Gateway's own "Login has completed" log line (up to 60s) instead of guessing a fixed delay; `restartIbkrGatewayOnVps.ts`'s SSH timeout bumped 90s → 120s to match. Deployed to the VPS by Marcelo (Claude's auto-mode classifier blocks writes/reads over the VPS SSH connection — even `docker logs`/`diff` — so this needs either a settings rule or Marcelo running it; same open gap as `feedback_classifier_blocks_destructive_vps_commands` in memory).

**2) Real bug: Pulse silently went stale during the ~30-60s Gateway restart and never recovered without a manual refresh.** Root cause was backend-side, not the frontend's fault: `streamLiveGreeks`/`streamLivePrices` (backing the `greeks`/`pnl` multiplexer subscriptions Pulse and Positions both use) only waited on the caller's abort signal once streaming started — nothing observed the IBKR socket actually dying, so a Gateway restart left the producer's promise hanging forever. Meanwhile the multiplexer's connection-level heartbeat kept the SSE transport looking perfectly healthy, so the frontend's (otherwise solid) `EventSource` reconnect logic never had anything to react to — a silent hang dressed up as a live connection. Separately, even if that end/error frame had arrived, the multiplexer client deleted a failed/ended subscription permanently and only called the caller's `onError` — Pulse doesn't pass one, so nothing would have resubscribed anyway.

**Fixed, three pieces:**
- Backend: new `waitForAbortOrGatewayDisconnect` (`src/ibkr/requestMarketData.ts`) — resolves on abort OR a real IBKR disconnect/code-1100 "connectivity lost" notice, so a Gateway restart now makes the producer return and the multiplexer send a proper `end` frame. Wired into both `streamLiveGreeks` and `streamLivePrices` (covers `greeks`, `pnl`, and anything else built on those two).
- Frontend: the multiplexer client (`src/api/streamMultiplexer.ts`) now re-subscribes a subscription (same id, backoff 1s→30s) on `error`/`end` instead of dropping it forever — `onError` is still called for any page that wants a visible indicator, but recovery no longer depends on the caller wiring one up.
- Frontend defense-in-depth: the backend's 20s heartbeat is now a real JSON frame (was an invisible SSE comment `: ping`), so the client can detect a connection that's silently wedged at the transport level (server process stuck, nothing at all arriving) and force a reconnect — a case neither of the above two fixes would catch.
- Not yet extended to `streamOrderLegQuote.ts`/`streamTickerDetail.ts`/`streamSignalsOptionQuotes.ts`/`fetchOptionChain.ts`/`fetchTickerOverview.ts`, which have the same `signal.addEventListener("abort", ...)`-only wait pattern — out of scope for this fix (not reported as broken), flagged here in case the same silent-stale symptom shows up on those screens.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean both repos, full suite 436/445 passing (9 pre-existing unrelated failures in `captureOptionQuoteBatch.test.ts`, confirmed failing on `main` before this change too). Not yet exercised live against a real Gateway restart — next real restart (or the VPS script fix above triggering one) is the first live test of the reconnect path specifically.

## Pulse chart history survives a refresh (built + verified 2026-09-23)
Same session as above: Marcelo noticed Pulse's Unrealised P&L / Profit Probability charts went blank on a manual refresh too — expected, since 2026-09-13's design deliberately kept no intraday history (client-side-only rolling sample, starts empty every load). Reversed that tradeoff for just the two series the charts actually plot (not raw IBKR ticks): new tables `pulse_unrealized_pnl_samples` (per position) and `pulse_profit_probability_samples` (per option leg), written by `src/lib/pulseChartSampleCollector.ts` — a side-channel the existing `streamPnlHandler`/`streamGreeksHandler` call into with their already-computed values on every update (no new IBKR calls; goes idle the moment nobody has Pulse open). One batched upsert per 60s window (same debounce-and-batch pattern as `priceService.ts`'s `recordStockPrices`, after today's own pool-exhaustion incident from *not* doing that), self-pruning to a rolling 8h window on each flush. New `GET /positions/pulse-chart-history` backfills both series (open positions only) for `PulsePage.tsx` to seed its charts from on mount, before the live SSE streams take over — merge is guarded so a slow backfill response never clobbers live data that already started arriving. `CHART_MAX_SAMPLES` bumped from 240 (4h) to 480 (8h) to match the new buffer — the client-side cap didn't previously match what the design intended to show.

Verified: migration applied to local dev DB, rolled back and re-applied cleanly; direct psql round-trip on both tables; collector's actual flush logic (not just raw SQL) exercised end-to-end against the real DB via a throwaway `tmp/` script — confirmed the 60s batched upsert produces the right row; `GET /positions/pulse-chart-history` curled directly (empty state, then with seeded rows — confirmed correct SUM/GROUP BY and per-position grouping); Playwright-verified in the browser against real seeded backfill rows — both charts rendered the exact historical shape inserted, then a reload with the rows deleted confirmed the empty-state path doesn't error. `tsc --noEmit` clean both repos, full suite 436/445 (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures as above). Not yet tested with two Pulse tabs open concurrently (the intended no-duplicate-write case) or against Juan's staging login.

## IBKR market-data line dedup + budget fold-in (started 2026-09-23, Phase 1 done)
Follow-on from the line-usage audit above: today, every screen opens its own IBKR `reqMktData` line per contract it watches, with **no sharing across concurrent viewers** — two people with Pulse open on the same 13 positions cost 32 lines, not 16, since `sharedReadConnection.ts` only shares the socket, never the subscription. Combined with `marketDataLineBudget.ts` (built earlier today) only covering the option-quote path, real usage is both uncounted and unbounded in the stock/greeks streams — audit found a **1-viewer worst case of 97-157 lines** against the 90-line budget / 100 IBKR hard cap, worse with any second viewer. Approved plan: (1) a shared, reference-counted subscription pool — one real IBKR line per contract, fanned out to every subscriber, always on `sharedLiveConnection` (fixed REALTIME) rather than the toggling read connection; (2) fold every currently-unbudgeted stream (Pulse/Positions, Dashboard exposure, Price Performance, Trade Alerts, Signals list) through it and into the reservation budget; (3) leave the nightly chain-capture job as-is (already budgeted, already snapshot-mode, dedup wouldn't help a one-shot sweep).

**Phase 1 built and verified**: `src/ibkr/stockPriceSubscriptionPool.ts` — `subscribeToPooledStockPrice(symbol, onUpdate)` ref-counts subscribers per symbol, issues exactly one `reqMktData` for a brand-new symbol, fans out ticks to every subscriber, and — unlike every other live stream in the app — **survives a Gateway restart with no caller-visible interruption**: on `EventName.disconnected` every entry is marked invalidated (not deleted) so subscribers stay registered, and a retry loop re-issues `reqMktData` for all of them the moment `sharedLiveConnection` reconnects, with nobody needing to resubscribe. Reserves against `marketDataLineBudget.ts` as one coarse `stockPricePool` holder sized to the live symbol count. `streamPooledStockPrices()` is a drop-in-shaped wrapper for SSE handlers; migrated Price Performance's `/current-prices/stream` (`routes/pricePerformance.ts`) off `streamLivePrices` onto it as the first real consumer — chosen first since it's stock-only, shortlist-wide, no P&L/greeks math on top, and today's biggest unarbitrated single line count (26).

**Bug found and fixed by the same live test that validated dedup**: two concurrent Price Performance tabs opened together then closed together left the `stockPricePool` reservation at `lines: 1` instead of 0 — concurrent unsubscribe calls (20 symbols × 2 tabs closing in the same tick) each fired an unserialized `reserveMarketDataLines` write for the same holder, and an out-of-order completion left a stale count. Same race class as `priceService.ts`'s `recordStockPrices` incident from earlier today. Fixed with the identical debounce-and-serialize pattern (one write in flight per holder, always reading the current count when it actually runs, not when it was scheduled).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 436/445 (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Live-tested against the real dev Gateway with two concurrent curl streams to `/price-performance/current-prices/stream`: both received identical live ticks, DB reservation showed exactly one `stockPricePool` row at `lines: 20` (not 40) the whole time both were open, and `lines: 0`/no row after both closed (post-fix; pre-fix reproduced the stale-count bug directly). Playwright-verified the actual Price Performance screen renders correctly end-to-end through the new path ("Live prices streaming", every row priced, no console errors).

**Phase 2 done and verified (same session).** Generalized the pool to cover option contracts too (`pricePool.ts`, renamed from `stockPriceSubscriptionPool.ts`), added a matching `greeksPool.ts` for option greeks (ticks 13/83), and migrated every remaining unbudgeted consumer: Positions/Pulse's `streamPnlHandler` and `streamGreeksHandler` (`routes/positions.ts`), Dashboard/Risk&Limits exposure (`lib/positionExposure.ts`), Trade Alerts (`routes/tradeAlerts.ts`), and the Signals screen + ticker-modal stock line (`streams/signalsProducers.ts`, one dependency-injection swap covers both). Resolved the FROZEN-vs-pooling tension by dropping the real FROZEN snapshot request entirely (a pooled REALTIME-only subscription can't ask for one without breaking dedup) and relying on each caller's own pre-existing fallback (DB last-known-price for stock, `position_pnl_snapshots`/`position_leg_greeks_snapshots`/entry-price for options) for a still-null first reading — the wrapper still waits a 3s settle grace period (same value as the old `frozenGraceMs`) before declaring its first reading final, so the UX timing is unchanged; only a quiet option contract's very first paint on a still-cold pool entry may show a fallback value instead of a true last-trade price for a few seconds, self-correcting the moment a live tick or another viewer's already-warm subscription arrives. `fetchLivePrices`/`fetchLiveGreeks`'s own ONE-SHOT (non-streaming) functions are untouched — pooling only applies to persistent subscriptions.

**Exhaustively tested**: `tsc --noEmit` and full suite clean on every migrated file (baseline unchanged: same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Live against the real dev Gateway: two concurrent `pnl` + two concurrent `greeks` streams on the actual 13 open positions/10 legs (7 option) showed `pricePool: 10` and `greeksPool: 7` — not 20/14 — with both viewers receiving identical live numbers; all reservations released to 0 within seconds of both streams closing. Playwright-verified every migrated screen individually (Pulse, Positions, Dashboard, Trade Alerts, Signals, Price Performance) — real data, correct settle-then-populate timing, zero console errors on any of them. Then opened **6 tabs simultaneously** (Price Performance, two Pulse tabs, Dashboard, Signals, home) to simulate the worst-case multi-viewer scenario from the earlier audit: **`pricePool: 28` + `greeksPool: 7` = 35 total lines** — against a pre-fix estimate of 97-157 lines for even a *single* viewer. Closing every tab released both reservations to 0.

**One anomaly observed once, not reproduced**: a single run of the 4-concurrent-stream close test left `pricePool`/`greeksPool` reservations stuck at their prior (not-yet-released) counts for over 10s; re-running the identical scenario twice more released cleanly within 3-4s both times. Root cause not pinned down (suspect transient DB pool contention from concurrent test tooling, not a logic bug — the serialized-reconcile fix from Phase 1 was already in place for both pools). Not a hard risk either way: every reservation carries a 90s TTL and self-expires the moment nothing renews it, so a stuck row can never block the budget for more than ~90s even in the worst case. Worth a note if it recurs, not worth chasing further right now.

**Still deliberately outside the pools**: option-quote consumers that already went through `marketDataLineBudget.ts` directly (Ticker Detail's live chain, Order Review's leg quote, Signals' ticker-modal option quotes, the nightly chain-capture batches) — all transient/snapshot-mode, single-viewer by construction, already correctly budgeted; pooling would add complexity for near-zero payoff there.

## IBKR market-data pools merged into one centralized pool (2026-09-24)
Marcelo wanted full system-wide dedup (not just per-pool) and was explicit that build cost wasn't a concern, only correctness (wasted lines, wrong data) and performance. Two corrections that came out of this: (1) "Ticker Detail's live chain is a snapshot" was wrong — checked the actual code and it's a persistent `live` mode subscription held open for the modal's life, same for Order Review's leg quote and Signals' ticker-modal quotes; only the nightly capture job is genuinely bounded/one-shot. (2) Found that `pricePool.ts` and `greeksPool.ts` were *already* double-subscribing every option leg Pulse/Positions watches — IBKR sends bid/ask/last AND computed greeks on one plain `reqMktData` call (empty generic tick list), so having two separate pools cost 2 lines per option leg where 1 would do, even before Ticker Detail entered the picture.

**Built `src/ibkr/marketDataPool.ts`** — the one place in the app that ever opens a live (non-order-execution, non-nightly-capture) `reqMktData` subscription. One line per contract, always requesting the full field set (last/bid/ask/delta/gamma/vega/theta/IV/underlying) for every option contract regardless of what the *first* subscriber needed — this is what makes a later subscriber wanting different fields (e.g. Ticker Detail joining a contract Pulse already warmed for price-only) work correctly without a second subscription. `pricePool.ts`/`greeksPool.ts` became thin field-projecting wrappers over this core, keeping their exact external signatures — **zero changes needed** to any already-migrated consumer (positions.ts, positionExposure.ts, tradeAlerts.ts, signalsProducers.ts, pricePerformance.ts). New `pooledOptionQuotes.ts` provides the `OptionQuote`-shaped (bid/ask/last/greeks together) wrapper for the three newly-migrated consumers: Ticker Detail's chain + spot price (`streamTickerDetail.ts`), Order Review's leg quote (`streamOrderLegQuote.ts`), Signals' ticker-modal quotes (`streamSignalsOptionQuotes.ts`). Deliberately did NOT touch `fetchOptionChain.ts` itself (its `fetchQuotesForContracts`/`quoteOptionChain` `live` mode is now dead code for these three callers, left in place) — another session was actively mid-edit in that exact file throughout this work (confirmed via `ListAgents`); the one-shot, non-`live` callers of `fetchQuotesForContracts` (trade-alert generation/refresh, `streamPositionQuote.ts`'s New Position form) are untouched and correctly out of scope — genuinely one-shot, no persistent subscription to dedup.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and full suite clean (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Cross-consumer dedup proven directly: a `greeksPool`-shaped subscriber and a `pooledOptionQuotes`-shaped subscriber on the *same* DELL 560P contract received byte-identical delta values updating in lockstep (via a throwaway `tmp/` script, since dev's `option_chain_expiry_strikes` table is empty for every ticker — pre-existing dev-data gap, unrelated to this work, confirmed by checking the table directly; blocked live UI testing of the chain/Signals-ticker-modal specifically, though the underlying mechanism they both call was proven directly). Playwright-verified Pulse, Positions, and Ticker Detail's modal (DELL: spot price, greeks, P&L all live and correct, chain section fails gracefully with a typed error instead of crashing) — zero console errors throughout. Re-ran the Gateway-disconnect test against the merged pool (same method as before: force this dev process's own IBKR socket closed via a temporary debug route, removed after use) — pnl + greeks streams kept flowing before and after, reservation held at a stable count (28, reflecting real concurrent Positions+Ticker Detail+Signals usage from the test session) throughout, never zeroed or doubled.

**Not live-tested**: Order Review's leg quote and Signals' ticker-modal quotes specifically through their actual UI paths (both blocked by the same empty-chain-table dev-data gap — Order Review needs a pending order, Signals' ticker modal didn't open without chain data). Their underlying mechanism (`streamPooledOptionQuotes`) is the exact same one proven directly against DELL 560P, and their wrapper code is a small, mechanical adaptation of the same pattern already verified for `streamOrderLegQuote`'s single-contract case — reasonably high confidence, but flagging the gap rather than claiming full UI coverage. Worth a real check next time `option_chain_expiry_strikes` has data (next nightly capture run, or ask Juan/staging where the table isn't empty).

**Gateway-restart resilience — live-tested 2026-09-24, passed.** Rather than restart the real shared VPS Gateway (would have disrupted two other concurrently-active sessions on IBKR work), added a temporary dev-only debug route that force-closed just this dev server's own IBKR socket — the exact `EventName.disconnected` trigger a real Gateway restart produces from the app's side, without touching the shared container. Two rounds: (1) one `pnl` + one `greeks` stream, forced disconnect mid-stream — both streams stayed open throughout (never errored/closed), live values kept changing before and after (confirmed via real number changes, not just heartbeats), reservations stayed at `pricePool: 10`/`greeksPool: 7` the whole time (never zeroed, never doubled). (2) Two concurrent viewers on each stream (the actual "you and Juan both watching Pulse" scenario) plus the same forced disconnect — all 4 streams recovered in lockstep with byte-identical data on both viewers of each pair, reservations unchanged throughout. Debug route removed after testing (`git diff --stat` confirms `systemHealth.ts` is back to its exact pre-test state).

## Pulse Gateway card: reconnects folded into Uptime, IBKR line usage added (2026-09-24)
Plain language: the Gateway box on IORIO Pulse now reads "Uptime 1h 42m (0 reconnects)" instead of spending a row on reconnects, and gains two rows — **IBKR live connections** (how many market-data lines the app has open with IBKR right now) and **Reserved lines** (how many of the 90-line budget are booked, including the scheduled scans' priority bookings). Both were asked for by Marcelo.

Caveat agreed before building: the Gateway worker on the VPS handles orders/account/positions only and never opens market-data lines, so these two numbers are not Gateway-worker stats. "Live connections" is the web dyno's centralized pool (`marketDataPool.ts`): entries with a real IBKR reqId, so paused/unsubscribed contracts don't count (chosen over `subscriberCount`, which counts screens and double-counts shared contracts, and over `contractCount`, which counts pooled contracts even when the budget has paused them — locally with `IBKR_MARKET_DATA_LINES_ENABLED=false` it reads 0 while 13 contracts sit paused). "Reserved" is the sum of active rows in `ibkr_market_data_line_reservations` (new `currentMarketDataLineReservationTotal()`), served by `GET /system-health/gateway` as `marketDataLineCount` / `reservedLineCount`.

Verified: endpoint curled (`marketDataLineCount: 0`, `reservedLineCount: 0` on dev), both repos `tsc` clean, Pulse Playwright-checked at 1440 and 1920 — each row on one line, no overflow, no console errors beyond the SSE drop from `tsx watch` restarting. **Pre-existing, not fixed:** at phone width (390px) the entire Pulse page is cramped on committed code — KPI tiles clip to "$1,0", every diagram node is ~70–130px wide with subtitles cut off — so the new rows clip exactly like the old ones. Needs a page-level phone layout decision, separate from this change.

## Staging outage 2026-09-24 (market hours): Signals best-line loop + abort-listener leak (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** From 13:30 UTC (US market open) the staging API froze within minutes of anyone opening the Signals screen: every request timed out, the screen showed "Live data disconnected", and restarting the dyno only bought a few minutes. The Signals screen keeps one live IBKR price line on each ticker's best contract and moves that line whenever another contract looks better. The line's own live price could make a *different* contract look better (the others were still priced from the 10:00 snapshot), so the line bounced back and forth thousands of times a second, and every bounce left a small piece of bookkeeping behind. The server spent all its time bouncing and ran out of memory. Production was never affected — it runs a build from 2026-09-21, before this code existed. Nothing for Juan to do.

**Technical record.**
- Introduced by `4e68627` (Day Signals, staging v55 at 05:44 UTC). The first market session with it was 2026-09-24: memory climbed from exactly 13:30 UTC, H12 storm from 13:47, `R14 Memory quota exceeded` at ~514 MB on the 512 MB Basic dyno.
- Diagnosed empirically on the live dyno's V8 inspector (`heroku ps:exec` → `kill -USR1 <pid>` → `heroku ps:forward 9229` → Chrome DevTools Protocol from a local script). Gotcha: `ps:forward` drops every forwarded connection after 60 s, so a full heap snapshot or forced GC of a swapped heap never completes over it — use sampling profiles that fit the window. Retained-allocation sampling (42 s): 100% of retained bytes from `syncBestLine` / `childAbort` (`AbortSignal`, listener `Map`, `addEventListener`). CPU profile (20 s): 84% self time inside `EventTarget.addEventListener` under `childAbort`, 99.7% inclusive under the option-quote callback — Node's duplicate-listener scan is O(listeners), so the leak made each flip slower (quadratic).
- Two defects in `src/streams/signalsProducers.ts`: (1) `childAbort` registered an `abort` listener on the parent subscription signal that was never removed when a line was retired; (2) moving the line cleared `liveQuotes`, so the next score compared the new line's live quote against the old line's snapshot quote and flipped straight back — all in microtasks, starving the event loop (dead SSE heartbeats → the toast).
- Fix: `childAbort` unhooks its parent listener when the child aborts; `TickerState.liveQuotes` is a per-contract map that survives line moves (readings of retired contracts are dropped when that ticker's day quotes reload), so a move is judged live-vs-live and settles. Regression test drives the exact flip sequence and asserts the parent signal's `abort` listener count is flat across two line moves.
- Also in the same push: `npm start` runs Node with `--max-old-space-size=384`. The probe showed heapTotal 475 MB with 286 MB live under Node's default 4.5 GB limit — V8 lets a 512 MB dyno drift into swap before it bothers to collect; prod gets this on its next promotion.
- Verification (2026-09-24, staging v63 = `78abebd`, booted 15:02 UTC): API answering instantly again — `/system-health/web-dyno` at 89 req/min with 4 multiplexed stream connections including a live `signalsScreen`; Signals shows "Live prices connected"; no further H12/R14. Node's `--max-old-space-size=384` is in the same release.
- Follow-ups from the same afternoon: (1) Pulse's Gateway card showed "Reserved lines —" after the API deploy because the API renamed the field to `priorityReservedLineCount` (`104188c`) while the staging front end was still on `0ed9762`; pushing the app's `353ef81` fixed the skew. (2) The Day Signals loop reserves its 10 lines with `priority: true` for the whole session, so the top bar's "Live data restricted" (driven by `loadMarketDataLineRestriction`) was on all day. Decision (Marcelo 2026-09-24): the loop is normal operation, not a restriction — `/environment/details` now excludes holder `daySignalsLoop` (`excludeHolders`), so the banner only appears while the chain capture or the trade-alert scan actually holds lines, with its existing hover/focus tooltip explaining the shed rules; `/system-health/gateway` keeps the factual priority count (the card's "(10 reserved)" during the loop).

## Day Signals alert flooding — notification hysteresis + per-contract cooldown (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** Marcelo reported the upgrade alerts (Telegram, toast, Pulse Latest Events) firing far too often for the same contract. Confirmed on staging: HOOD's $116 cash-secured put alone sent 8 "weak → good" alerts in 11 minutes. The contract wasn't actually improving — its net Edge was hovering right on the weak/good line (5.0–7.5vp against a 5vp cut point), so ordinary bid/ask noise flipped it back below the line (a silent downgrade, not notified) and back above it (notified again) on nearly every ~1-2 minute refresh cycle. The original design's comment assumed a refresh cycle "takes minutes, so flapping is not a concern" — it doesn't hold once cycles run that fast.

**Fix (both approved 2026-09-24, Marcelo):**
1. **Notification hysteresis** (`clearsNotificationHysteresis`, `daySignalsNotifications.ts`) — a grade transition only notifies if net Edge clears the grade's own cut point (Formula 3j: 0/5/10vp for weak/good/strong) by a **2vp margin** (so weak→good needs ≥7vp, good→strong ≥12vp, avoid→weak ≥2vp). The grade shown on screen is unchanged — `gradeForNetEdge` still grades at the bare cut points; this only gates whether an upward transition is convincing enough to notify. `strongCutVolatilityPoints`/`goodCutVolatilityPoints` exported from `signalCandidates.ts` so the two files share one source of truth for the cut points.
2. **Per-contract cooldown** (`daySignalsLoop.ts`, `daySignalsNotificationCooldownMs = 10 * 60_000`) — once a contract notifies, it stays quiet for 10 minutes regardless of further grade movement, purely by elapsed time (no requirement that it first fall back to a lower grade). Keyed per exact contract (ticker+expiry+strike+right for new-trade candidates, legId+expiry+strike+right for rolls — both scoped separately by a `signal:`/`roll:` prefix). In-memory only (`lastNotifiedAtByKey` on the `DaySignalsLoop` instance), not persisted — a mid-window loop restart resets it, an accepted tradeoff against a DB round trip per check.
3. Applies to both Signal upgrades and Roll Signal upgrades identically.

Grade recording to `day_signal_quotes.last_grade` / `day_signal_roll_grades` is unconditional as before — only the notification is gated, so the next cycle's `isGradeUpgrade` comparison always sees the true last grade, not a suppressed one. Tests: `daySignalsNotifications.test.ts` (new, unit tests on `clearsNotificationHysteresis`/`isGradeUpgrade`), `daySignalsLoop.test.ts` (new cases: hysteresis withholds a marginal Weak notification while still recording the grade; cooldown suppresses a repeat within 10 minutes and allows it again after; existing roll-upgrade test updated since the one marginal roll in its fixture no longer notifies). Full suite (572 tests) and `tsc` clean.

**Live-verified on staging 2026-09-24 (market hours, real data).** Pushed to staging (v66) and checked against real signal-upgrade activity: every `signal_upgraded` notification recorded after the deploy cleared its required hysteresis margin (weak→good ≥7vp, good→strong ≥12vp, avoid→weak ≥2vp) — zero violations across dozens of real upgrades (BMNR, HOOD, COIN), including several right at the boundary (7.04vp, 7.23vp, 2.0vp). Cooldown: the one contract that repeated in a clean, restart-free window (BMNR $28.50 covered call) waited 17m15s between notifications, well clear of the 10-minute window. One apparent 3m23s "violation" turned out to be a false alarm — a staging deploy (pushing the Telegram-suspend commit below) restarted the dyno in between, which resets the in-memory cooldown map by design, not a bug.

## Order Review calendar-warning/risk-free-rate persistence + Live Quote instant seed (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** Two related Order Review fixes. First: the "economic events before expiry" warning banner (and the probability-of-profit rate behind it) used to flash on screen and then vanish — it was computed when the order was created but never actually saved, so the very next refresh showed nothing. Second: the "Live Quote" card on Order Review used to sit on a spinner for a moment every time, even though the app usually already had that same quote a second earlier from wherever the trade was picked (an option chain row, a Signals candidate). It now paints instantly from that already-known quote.

**Technical record.**
- `calendarWarning`/`riskFreeRate` persistence (api, `248ad4b`): both were computed at order-creation time but only ever returned on the POST response, never written to the `order_requests` row. The `order_status` notification fired before that computation even started, so the SSE listener's own `GET /orders/:id` could beat the persist and overwrite the panel with nothing. New migration adds `calendar_warning`/`risk_free_rate` columns; both are now persisted before the notification goes out.
- Live Quote instant seed (app, `7a04ef4`): new `initialQuote` prop on `OrderReviewPanel`, display-only — the compliance/signalLimits gate still waits on the real stream's first tick, so Confirm can't unlock early off stale seed data. `TickerDetailModal` passes its existing live quote; `SignalsTickerModal` maps the selected candidate/roll into the narrower seed shape (no gamma/last of its own).
- **Live-verified 2026-09-24 (market hours, staging, paper account).** Placed a real 1-contract BMNR $27 cash-secured put with a genuine calendar warning present (Trump/Xi summit + Durable Goods Orders before the Sep 25 expiry). Confirmed via direct DB query that `calendar_warning`/`risk_free_rate` persisted on the row, and the banner stayed visible across multiple refresh cycles instead of flashing away. Live Quote card painted bid/ask/spread/IV/delta/theta/vega instantly with no spinner. Order cancelled afterward for cleanup (confirmed `cancelled` status, IBKR order id 7 acknowledged the cancel).

## Stale-surface caveat folded into per-ticker model caveats (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** The Signals screen used to show a one-off "surface &lt;date&gt;" hover badge for a ticker scored on an old volatility surface, inconsistent with the click-to-explain "model" caveat badge used for every other per-ticker issue (short history, suspected split, etc.). It now renders through that same structured caveat list and popover.

**Technical record.**
- api `1c31833` / app `bd482d7`: new `stale_surface` caveat (status `waiting_on_next_run`) fires when a ticker's `snapshotDateIso` is non-null but doesn't match today; unit-tested (`signalsRoadmap.test.ts`).
- **Live-checked 2026-09-24 (staging), inconclusive on the specific caveat.** Every ticker with a failed capture today (QQQ, SMCI, SMH, IGV, INTC, LITE, MU, NBIS, PLTR, SPCX, TLT) has never had a successful capture at all, so they correctly show `no_snapshot` instead (that caveat takes priority). Did confirm the caveat UI plumbing itself works live: DRAM's `short_history` caveat renders correctly through the same structured popover, and no ticker shows a spurious caveat. `stale_surface` itself needs a ticker whose capture succeeds most days but fails on one particular day — none exist on staging right now, so this one is still unverified against real data.

Marcelo turned off Telegram delivery for Signal/Roll upgrade alerts (`a98f1d1`) — the in-app toast and Pulse Latest Events still fire on every upgrade, Telegram just doesn't. `formatSignalUpgradeMessage`/`formatRollSignalUpgradeMessage` are unused but kept in place for that path if it's re-enabled. **Live-verified 2026-09-24:** watched several real upgrades (HOOD, COIN) toast correctly in-app during testing. Not verified: that no Telegram message actually went out — needs Marcelo to confirm nothing hit his phone during the test window (~16:06–16:19 UTC, 2026-09-24).

## Day Signals follows the price (built and tested 2026-09-29, pushed to staging; needs a market-hours check there)

**Plain language.** The 9:30 snapshot only stores the options that were out-of-the-money at the open. When a stock moved a lot afterwards (BE +14%), the puts you would now sell had no quotes and the chain showed dashes. Day Signals now re-checks each stock's live price every cycle and quotes the options that make sense at that price, dropping the ones that no longer do (the line count stays 10). If a stock moves far enough, it also re-picks which expiries are worth watching.

**Technical.** Per cycle: a spot pass over every pooled ticker, then per ticker `selectDaySignalContractSet` (`daySignalsContractSet.ts`) = the capture's `selectContractsToCapture` at the live spot, plus previous contracts within one strike step (buffer), plus open-leg contracts; dropped contracts' day quotes are deleted (`pruneDayQuotesOutsideSet`); tickers without a context, IV or spot stay on the snapshot's contracts. Re-rank trigger (approved formula): |spot - last-rank spot| / last-rank spot >= max(1%, 0.5 x ATM IV / sqrt(252)), max 3 per ticker per day, persisted in `day_signal_rerank_state` (migration `20260929000004`; one row per ticker per day, wiped by the seed; no row = reference is the 9:30 spot, so a restart neither repeats a re-rank nor resets the cap). Re-rank quotes the rule's contracts across all fitted expiries, scores with those live quotes, keeps the top 3 expiries by the seed rule plus held-leg expiries (`replaceTickerPoolExpiries`); no positive-Edge candidate keeps the current pool. `scoreTicker` now appends any day/live quote the snapshot lacks, so the chain and screens show them; legend text now "not in today's capture or refresh".
- A contract the loop starts quoting mid-day (stored quotes exist from an earlier cycle, so not the first cycle after the seed) counts as coming from Avoid: it can notify on its first grade, subject to the usual hysteresis and cooldown. Applies to roll replacements too. Expect a burst after a big move or a re-rank into a new expiry.
- **Follow-up 2026-09-29 (same day, after the first staging look):** BE was not in the 9:30 pool at all (the seed found no positive-Edge candidate at the open's prices), and the first version only watched pooled tickers, so it never re-ranked. The loop now also tracks every Signals-universe ticker with today's snapshot and no pool (`loadDayUnpooledTickers`): same spot pass and trigger, and a re-rank can create the pool (`replaceTickerPoolExpiries` now takes the snapshot id). If the re-rank still finds no positive-Edge expiry, the ticker stays unpooled and its chain keeps dashes near the price (open question for Marcelo: pool the best expiry anyway?).
- The Signals chain no longer shows in-the-money contracts (call strike below spot, put strike above): desktop blanks the cell, phone drops the row; a contract under an open order review stays visible. Frontend only, uses the live spot.
- **Lines only for what is on screen (2026-09-29/30, replaces the spot-following live set of 2026-09-29):** the Signals modal subscribes live option quotes only for contracts currently visible (chain rows, Opportunities rows, held-leg/roll rows; a row counts once at least half of it shows, no extra rows either side), via a new `signalsQuotes` stream the modal reopens 300 ms after the visible set settles; it returns those contracts re-scored at the live spot, which the modal overlays (order/filters stay on the ticker stream's values so a re-grade never reshuffles visible rows). `signalsTicker` holds no option lines now. Chain and Opportunities are each sized to 10 rows (~10 lines each while in view). Across the whole app, a browser tab hidden for 2 s unsubscribes every line-holding stream (greeks, pnl, exposure, price-performance, stock prices, all Signals streams) and resubscribes on return; notifications/presence and Pulse topology stay connected. "Active tab" = visible, not focused (two windows side by side both keep their lines; Marcelo OK'd).
- To check on staging: cycle time with the changed contract counts, that a real mover gets its new puts quoted and graded, the chain screen on desktop and phone.

## FV excluding earnings days — backtest, not adopted (2026-09-30)

**Plain language.** Checked whether the volatility forecast (FV) should skip the days right after earnings reports. It barely changes today's numbers (BE 117% → 114%, AAOI 121% → 116%, NBIS 115% → 110%), doesn't forecast better, and the stored earnings dates are unreliable for half the shortlist. The big negative Edge on BE/AAOI/NBIS/DRAM comes from genuinely large non-earnings moves over the last three months, not from earnings days. FV stays as it is.

**Technical.** `tmp/fvExEarningsBacktest.ts` (read-only, staging data, shortlist of 10; exclusion = report date + next trading day; my Yang-Zhang matches production exactly with nothing excluded). Windows with no earnings ahead (what Signals ranks): QLIKE difference ex − now +0.002/+0.003/+0.002 at h = 10/21/42, 95% block-bootstrap intervals all span 0; corr(IV−FV, IV−realized) 0.27/0.13/0.14 now vs 0.28/0.12/0.12 ex. Current FV over-forecasts realized vol by 9.4%/6.7%/3.9% (mean log ratio) in those windows; ex-earnings 6.0%/3.5%/1.2%. Calendar check: the listed date + next day catches the biggest move near the report in 18/20 INTC, 15/16 NBIS, 16/20 SMCI, but only 9–11/20 for AAOI, BE, COIN, HOOD and BMNR (BMNR's "earnings" days move less than an ordinary day — its dates are wrong); API Ninjas' timing fields are paywalled on our plan.

## secDefOptParams timeout investigation — QQQ/SMCI/SMH (resolved by a Gateway restart 2026-09-25; root cause still unknown)

**Plain language.** These three tickers couldn't capture an option chain on staging — refreshing showed a spinner, then reverted to 0 with a warning triangle. Traced it all the way down to IBKR's own Gateway software: it was silently refusing to answer this one type of request for these three tickers specifically, while working fine for every other ticker tested. Restarting the paper Gateway on the VPS cleared it immediately and all three now capture correctly, verified through the real app. We still don't know *why* it got into that state, so it could come back — if it does, a Gateway restart is the known fix.

**Technical record.**
- Confirmed with a standalone script that bypassed the app and IBC entirely: fresh connections straight to IBKR's paper Gateway got **zero** response (no data, no error, no end-of-stream marker, 45s wait) to `reqSecDefOptParams` for QQQ/SMCI/SMH specifically, while AAPL and HOOD resolved cleanly in under 4s each. SMCI's plain stock contract details resolved fine via `reqContractDetails` in under 1s, which rules out a bad/stale `ibkr_contract_id`.
- No server-side diagnostics were available to explain *why*: IBKR's audit trail and its optional "Create API Message Log" output are both written in an encrypted proprietary format (`.ibgzenc`) with no decryption tool available to us — enabling that logging (via a one-time VNC session opened on the paper Gateway container) was a dead end for root-causing this, and was reverted afterward (VNC removed, logging settings restored, generated log files deleted).
- Restarted the paper Gateway (`docker compose up -d ib-gateway-paper` on the VPS) — all three tickers immediately started resolving (SMCI 1.7s, QQQ 3.1s, SMH 2.1s direct; then confirmed through the actual staging app's refresh-option-chain endpoint end-to-end).
- Root cause remains unknown — looks like a wedged/stuck internal request state in IBKR's Gateway process for specific symbols. No app code changes were needed; temporary diagnostic logging added to `fetchOptionChain.ts` (`e32a06a`) was removed again (`472a6ba`) once the fix was confirmed.
- **Side finding, still open:** while investigating, hit a separate, previously-unexplained "We are having trouble loading your settings, they may be corrupted" dialog on Gateway restart (Marcelo: "we've had this issue several times"). Added `stop_grace_period: 90s` to both Gateway services in `/opt/ibkr/docker-compose.yml` on the VPS on the theory that Docker's 10s default was SIGKILLing TWS before it finished writing its settings file — but the dialog **recurred on the next restart anyway with the fix in place**, so that theory is disproven/unconfirmed. Verified the Gateway stays functional even with the dialog showing (API connects fine, option chains resolve), so it's cosmetic for now but unresolved.
- `/opt/ibkr` on the VPS has no git history — a manual `.bak` copy of the pre-change `docker-compose.yml` was made before editing (`docker-compose.yml.bak.20260925001127`) and is the only record of the original config. Open question raised but not yet decided: whether to put VPS infra config (this docker-compose setup) under git for real version history instead of manual `.bak` files going forward.

## Close form: live prices, fill priority, wheel cycle P&L (built 2026-09-28, pushed to staging; live path still to verify in market hours)
Plain-language: every row in Dashboard "Needs Attention" now has a Close button (same close form as Positions). The close form now uses only live prices, shows the ticker's wheel cycle P&L live, lets you pick the fill priority (Patient/Normal/Urgent) up front, and refuses to place a close when the market is closed, live quotes are missing, or the ticker's cycle data is inconsistent.
- New SSE `GET /positions/:id/close-live/stream` (`routes/positionCloseLive.ts`, logic in `lib/closeLiveState.ts`, 12 unit tests): live bid/ask per open leg from the shared pool, live cycle P&L via `deriveCycles` with the live stock last and the position's option leg marked at the bid/ask mid, and a `blockReason`. Live = regular session open + a real bid AND ask (the pool's stock fallback only fills `last`).
- Frontend: `ClosePositionModal` (both forms), `FillPriorityPicker`, Close button on Needs Attention rows; the picked priority seeds the order-review dropdown.
- Gating is UI-only; `POST /positions/:id/close` and the Genosuke tools are unchanged. The Signals/Roll forms keep their own inline priority buttons.
- Not testable locally: real IBKR quotes (market closed, worker offline); the live path was driven with a fake stream. Verify on staging in market hours.

## Positions: Cycle P&L column, tabs and Open/Closed filter removed (built and tested locally 2026-09-28, not pushed)
Plain-language: the Positions table has a new Cycle P&L column, the profit or loss of the whole wheel cycle of that symbol, calculated with the same price the row shows so it lines up with the other figures. The footer adds each symbol once. The All / Covered Calls / Cash-Secured Puts tabs and the Open/Closed toggle are gone; the page now shows open positions only.
- New `GET /positions/cycles/marks` (`positionCycleMarks.ts`, `lib/cycleLiveMarks.ts`, 7 tests): per open ticker the stored-mark cycle total, ledger shares, mark price and each open option position's stored premium mark. Live figure in the browser (`lib/cycleLivePnl.ts`): total + shares x (row Price - mark price) + sum (live unrealized premium P&L - stored mark); a test proves it equals re-deriving the cycle with those marks.
- A mark that isn't available stays at the stored value (last daily close / nightly snapshot), with an "As of" tooltip when the row has no price; a flagged cycle shows a dash with the reason.
- Closed positions are no longer viewable on this page.

## Pulse phone layout + mobile Pulse link (built and tested locally 2026-09-28, not pushed)

*In plain terms:* Pulse now has a proper phone layout instead of the desktop grid crushed into a narrow screen. Approved mockup (Proposal B): [Pulse Mobile Proposals](https://claude.ai/artifact/RFyVDjKoQkV1wzCHULZkCY). At ≤700px wide the page scrolls, the two header rows stay pinned, KPIs sit in a 2×2 grid over a slim allocation bar, and each desktop panel becomes a collapsible with a one-line summary, in the order Charts, Positions, Top Signals, Trades, Latest Events, Systems (Positions and Latest Events open by default; open/closed remembered per panel in localStorage). Systems' collapsed header shows one LED per node and a live pulse flashes both ends of its edge. Trades/Events show 8 rows then "Show N more".

- App repo: `hooks/useMediaQuery.ts`, `components/pulse/PulsePhoneLayout.tsx`, `PulseCollapsiblePanel.tsx`, `PnlSparkline.tsx`, `pulseEdges.ts` (edge list moved out of `TopologyMap.tsx`); `PulsePage.tsx` renders every tile/row/node card once and hands them to either layout; phone CSS block at the end of `PulsePage.css`.
- Decisions (Marcelo): standalone home-screen web app meta added to `index.html` (`viewport-fit=cover`, `apple-mobile-web-app-capable`, black-translucent status bar) with safe-area padding on the Pulse header/bottom and the mobile navbar; phone Pulse link in the mobile badges row opens in the same tab, and Pulse's brand mark links back to `/`; Positions summary exposure % = Σ capital at risk ÷ account value; node LED rules: DB worst of its bands, Gateway red when not connected / amber when reconnects or in-flight orders hit warn, IBKR red while account data fails, others green once their health call answered.
- Verified with Playwright at 440×956 and 1440×900 (desktop unchanged); `tsc` and `oxlint` clean. Not yet checked on a real iPhone (safe-area insets are 0 in Chrome).

## Pluto — autonomous trading agent (design discussion started 2026-09-28; branch-only, no code yet)
Plain-language: Pluto is a new bot that will trade Iorio's Signals on its own, on the staging paper account only, for as long as it takes to prove it works. Iorio keeps producing every number; a language model makes the final "trade this one or not" call; code checks that call against hard limits before any order exists. Genosuke stays as the Telegram chat bot and is not being extended.
- **Design of record** lives in the guiding artifact (Phase 4–5 section, "Pluto"). Decisions so far (Marcelo, 2026-09-28): staging-only via an existence layer (dedicated `agent` Procfile process scaled to 0 on prod + env var + `APP_ENVIRONMENT` check); modes off/on only, unsupervised from day one on paper; the model picks a candidate by id and never authors order fields; code sizes; separate Pluto capital budget; scope = opens, rolls (Formula 3j) and constrained closes (unstructured shares at positive cycle P&L, never at a loss; short-leg buybacks only when locking a profit, formula pending sign-off); pause + pause-and-cancel buttons, resume behind a confirm; default paused after a deploy or crash loop, not after Heroku's daily cycle; breakers wait for a human; two LLM calls must agree, second call only when the first says trade; event-gated LLM calls (candidate-set hash), not fixed cadence; OpenRouter (model switchable by config); no shortlist notes in the prompt; per-ticker `bot_enabled` flag on the shortlist, default off; Juan and Marce both can pause/resume; every pass also logs the deterministic Edge $ top pick for comparison; pessimistic bid-fill P&L logged next to the paper fill.
- **Platform gaps to close on `main` first, before Pluto** (all benefit human trading too): no kill switch (needs a DB flag checked in `tradingGate`, in the worker before `placeOrder`, and by Pluto); close gate (`closeLiveState`) and delta-band compliance enforced in the browser only; risk limits enforced only for Signals-originated orders, `strategy_settings.max_*` never enforced; order states lie (`rejected` never written, IBKR `Inactive` ignored, rejection dropped unless the row is `submitted`, partial fill ends the day as `cancelled`); no surface max age; open interest/volume captured but not loaded into Signals.
- **Branch plan**: `pluto` branch in both repos, worked in git worktrees with their own `.env`/local DB/ports, `main` merged in weekly (never rebased), single local merge + push when ready; initial staging deploy with `agent` scaled to 0, full test battery, then scale to 1.
- Not started: decision-ledger schema, gate list, prompt contract, parameter defaults (all need sign-off), Pluto screen mockup.

## Short-dated surface fits and rolls on unscored legs (2026-09-30 to 2026-10-01, all committed locally, nothing pushed)
Plain language: a roll on a contract whose expiry had no usable volatility surface was blocked with a wrong message ("no quote"), and short-dated surfaces were being rejected far too often. Three changes: (1) the roll form now prices at the live quotes when the held leg has a quote but no score; (2) each expiry's surface uses the stock price the option quotes were taken at, not the price read before the capture; (3) the surface's forward is now the one the option market itself implies (put-call parity: median over near-ATM strikes with a usable call and put, within 5% of the forward, falling back to (2) when there are none). Re-fitting all 67 stored staging snapshots (619 slices) with the real code: rejected fits 60 → 4, none that were fine got worse.
- **Formula change (Marcelo approved 2026-09-30):** forward `F = K + (C_mid − P_mid)·e^(rT)` per strike, median across strikes; replaces the "spot minus PV of dividends" forward whenever a pair exists. New column `option_surface_fits.underlying_price` (migration `20260930000001`) stores the price the forward is anchored to; live scoring rescales the forward by `liveSpot / that price` (old fits: null → snapshot spot, as before). Stored fits are not backfilled; applies from the next nightly fit. A push to staging runs the migration in the release phase.
- **Capture now stores both sides near the money (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, committed):** the 10:00 capture also takes the in-the-money side of every strike within 3% of spot (`captureBothSidesWithinFractionOfSpot` in `optionChainCaptureWindow.ts`, opt-in argument of `selectContractsToCapture`; the Day Signals loop keeps the old rule). Before this, each expiry had exactly one call/put pair (the strike nearest spot), so the parity forward rested on a single pair. Estimate on staging data: about +5.5 contracts per expiry, +520 per night (+17%), +57 s on a ~340 s run at 50 lines; no extra market-data lines. The loop only sees the extra contracts where it falls back to the stored snapshot set (a ticker with no live context, or an expiry it cannot window).
- **Watch (first nightly runs after this ships):** the capture's job duration and settle stats; `select ... count(distinct strike) filter (both sides)` per expiry should be ~6 instead of 1; the spread of per-strike parity forwards within an expiry should be well below the 0.1-0.4% seen against other expiries. Earlier note: most expiries had one usable call/put pair

## Optional / future ideas (not being built unless requested)
- ~~Live-stream connection cap in dev~~ **Resolved as a side effect, corrected 2026-09-25.** This item (parked 2026-09-19) weighed several fixes for Chrome's 6-connection HTTP/1.1 cap in local dev, including "(c) multiplex every per-tab stream onto one SSE" as a days-not-hours future option. That option was since built for unrelated reasons — see "Stream multiplexing (built)" above, commit `4341611` — and per its own doc comment (`streamProtocol.ts`), one SSE connection per tab now carries every live subscription that tab has open. The original per-tab connection-count problem this whole entry was about is almost certainly moot as a result (not independently re-verified in dev since the multiplexer shipped).
- Full historical backtesting engine using purchased historical options data (10 years) — cost/ToS permitting. Lightweight forward-simulation (self-accumulated data only) is planned instead, not optional.
- IBKR chart embedding for open positions — superseded by the decision to build our own via lightweight-charts.
- **Adjustment-heavy strategies for Pluto, once fully tested (research only, 2026-09-29, nothing scoped or built)**: candidates picked specifically because they need frequent rule-based monitoring/adjustment a bot handles better than a human — iron condor active defense (roll the untested side for a credit once the tested short strike's delta crosses ~30-35), calendar spread rolling (weekly front-leg roll), diagonal spread rolling, delta-neutral gamma scalping (continuous rebalancing against a long straddle/strangle). 0DTE strategies discussed and explicitly deferred — gamma too explosive for a first automation target. All are new strategy types (code, not config) and none are decided; revisit only after Pluto is proven on CC/CSP.

- **Strategy badge standardisation (2026-09-20)**: one shared `StrategyBadge` (app `components/StrategyBadge.tsx`, palette in `lib/positionPnl.ts`): CC blue, CSP purple, N/S orange ("Unstructured" badge renamed N/S). Used by Positions, PositionCard, Ticker modal alerts, Trade Alerts, Trade Blotter, Cycle bucket badge, Dashboard events; Dashboard history chart and Pulse badges/allocation bar use the same Tabler colours. Spelled-out "Unstructured" text (tiles, tables, legends) not yet renamed — awaiting decision.
- **Shortlist Remove blocked while a position is open (2026-09-28)**: a ticker with an open position can't be removed from the shortlist. Plain-language: removing it would leave the live position without the roll scan and Recovery Path that read the shortlist. The Actions menu greys Remove out with the reason ("1 open position on this ticker. Close it before removing."), and `DELETE /shortlist/:id` refuses with 409 so Genosuke's `remove_shortlist_ticker` and stale tabs get the same answer. `GET /shortlist` rows carry `openPositionCount`; helpers `countOpenPositionsForTicker` / `describeOpenPositionsBlockingRemoval` in `lib/positionQueries.ts`; app gained a shared `pluralize` formatter.
